commute.
With reference to the diagrams (
3.1
) and (
3.2
), the equations in (
3
) provide endpoint
k
for
\phi
and
\psi
as well as strength, respectively, while the equations in (
3
) provide endpoints
1-k
for
\phi
and
\psi
, respectively.
∎

Note that strong
k
-oriented homotopy equivalences have better closure properties than strong
k
-oriented deformation retracts.
For example,
lemma

3.3
implies that strong
k
-oriented homotopy equivalences are closed under retracts.
Indeed, let
f
be a strong homotopy equivalence and
g
be a retract of
f
.
Then
\theta^{k}\mathbin{\hat{\otimes}}g
is a retract of
\theta^{k}\mathbin{\hat{\otimes}}f
since
\theta^{k}\mathbin{\hat{\otimes}}(-)\colon\cal{E}^{\to}\to(\cal{E}^{\to})^{\to}
is a functor and functors preserve retracts.
Since
f
is a strong homotopy equivalence,
\theta^{k}\mathbin{\hat{\otimes}}f
is a section.
But then
\theta^{k}\mathbin{\hat{\otimes}}g
is also a section (since sections are closed under retracts), and so
g
is a strong
k
-oriented homotopy equivalence.

Lemma 3.4
.

For every map
h\colon X\to Y
in
\cal{E}
, we have that
\delta^{k}\mathbin{\hat{\otimes}}h
is a strong
k
-oriented homotopy equivalence.

This is a diagram-chasing argument, which we outline in the case
k=0
.
Let us abbreviate
f=_{\operatorname{def}}\delta^{0}\mathbin{\hat{\otimes}}h\colon(I\otimes X)+_{X}Y\to I\otimes Y
.
We define
g
as the composite

\textstyle{I\otimes Y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\epsilon\otimes Y}
\textstyle{Y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{1}}
\textstyle{(I\otimes X)+_{X}Y\hbox to0.0pt{.\hss}}

It remains to define homotopies
\phi\colon g\mathbin{\circ}f\sim\operatorname{id}_{X}
and
\psi\colon f\mathbin{\circ}g\sim\operatorname{id}_{Y}
such that
f\mathbin{\circ}\phi=\psi\mathbin{\circ}(I\otimes f)
.
The homotopy
\psi\colon I\otimes I\otimes Y\to I\otimes Y
is defined as the connection
\psi=_{\operatorname{def}}c^{0}\otimes Y
.
The signature of the homotopy
\phi
can be equivalently stated as

\phi\colon(I\otimes I\otimes X)+_{I\otimes X}I\otimes Y\to(I\otimes X)+_{X}Y

since
I\otimes(-)
preserves pushouts.
We then define
\phi=(c^{0}\otimes X)+_{\epsilon\otimes X}(\epsilon\otimes Y)
.
Standard reasoning, using diagrams (
1.1
), (
1.2
), (
1.3
) and naturality, verifies that
\phi
and
\psi
have the correct endpoints and that the
0
-oriented homotopy equivalence
(f,g,\phi,\psi)
is strong.
∎

lemma

3.4
follows also from the more general
lemma

7.4
, for which we provide a more conceptual proof.

For our second step, we return to consider a suitable wfs
(\mathsf{Cof},\mathsf{TrivFib})
with generating set
\cal{I}
and the induced wfs
(\mathsf{TrivCof},\mathsf{Fib})
of
proposition

2.5
, with generating set
\cal{I}_{\otimes}
defined in (
2.3
).
We focus on the class
\mathsf{Cof}\cap S
,
i.e.
the class of cofibrations that are strong homotopy equivalences.
The next lemma relates them to the generating trivial cofibrations and to the trivial cofibrations.

Lemma 3.5
.

We have

(i)

\cal{I}_{\otimes}\subseteq\mathsf{Cof}\cap S
,

(ii)

\mathsf{Cof}\cap S\subseteq\mathsf{TrivCof}
.

For part (i), we have that
\cal{I}_{\otimes}\subseteq\mathsf{Cof}
since
\cal{I}\subseteq\mathsf{Cof}
and
\mathsf{Cof}
is closed under Leibniz product with
\delta^{k}
(condition (S4) in
definition

2.1
).
We also have
\cal{I}_{\otimes}\subseteq S

since
\delta^{k}\mathbin{\hat{\otimes}}f
is a strong
k
-oriented homotopy equivalence for every map
f
by
lemma

3.4
.
For part (ii), let
f\in\mathsf{Cof}\cap S
.
Since
f\in S
,
lemma

3.3
implies that
f
is a retract of
\delta^{k}\mathbin{\hat{\otimes}}f
for some
k\in\{0\,,1\}
.
Because
f\in\mathsf{Cof}
, we have
\delta^{k}\mathbin{\hat{\otimes}}f\in\mathsf{TrivCof}
by
corollary

2.9
.
The claim then follows since
\mathsf{TrivCof}
is closed under retracts.
∎

Remark 3.6
.

By
lemma

3.5
, we have that
\mathsf{Fib}={\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\smash{(\mathsf{Cof}\cap S)}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-43.0pt\hskip 43.0pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-43.0pt\hskip 43.0pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\smash{(\mathsf{Cof}\cap S)}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-43.0pt\hskip 43.0pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-43.0pt\hskip 43.0pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\smash{(\mathsf{Cof}\cap S)}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-31.13135pt\hskip 31.13135pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-31.13135pt\hskip 31.13135pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\smash{(\mathsf{Cof}\cap S)}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-25.41338pt\hskip 25.41338pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-25.41338pt\hskip 25.41338pt}}}}
,
i.e.
a map is a fibration if and only if it has the right lifting property with respect to the cofibrations that are strong homotopy equivalences.

The next lemma is the third main step of the proof of the Frobenius property for
(\mathsf{TrivCof},\mathsf{Fib})
.

Lemma 3.7
.

For
k\in\{0\,,1\}
, the pullback of a strong
k
-oriented homotopy equivalence along a fibration is a strong
k
-oriented homotopy equivalence.

Let
g\in S_{k}
,
p\in\mathsf{Fib}
, and consider a pullback diagram

(3.24)

\begin{gathered}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 11.00002pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-6.75pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-11.00002pt\raise-15.66666pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.83888pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bar{g}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-24.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 31.53471pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 10.18231pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-10.18231pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\lower 0.0pt\hbox{\lower 0.0pt\hbox{\line@@}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\lower 0.0pt\hbox{\lower-5.0pt\hbox{\line@@}}}\hbox{}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 31.53471pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{B\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 38.57811pt\raise-15.66666pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.8264pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{g}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 38.57811pt\raise-24.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-7.53471pt\raise-31.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.22787pt\raise-36.5208pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.8264pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{p}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 31.56422pt\raise-31.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 31.56422pt\raise-31.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{Y\hbox to0.0pt{.\hss}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{gathered}

We wish to show that
\bar{g}\in S_{k}
.
Since
g\in S_{k}
,
\theta^{k}\mathbin{\hat{\otimes}}g\colon g\to\delta^{k}\mathbin{\hat{\otimes}}g
has a retraction
\rho\colon\delta^{k}\mathbin{\hat{\otimes}}g\to g
.
We show that
\theta^{k}\mathbin{\hat{\otimes}}\bar{g}\colon\bar{g}\to\delta^{k}\mathbin{\hat{\otimes}}\bar{g}
has a retraction
\bar{\rho}\colon\delta^{k}\mathbin{\hat{\otimes}}\bar{g}\to\bar{g}
.
We define
\bar{\rho}
so that the diagram

\textstyle{\bar{g}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\theta^{k}\mathbin{\hat{\otimes}}\bar{g}}
\scriptstyle{\sigma}
\textstyle{\delta^{k}\mathbin{\hat{\otimes}}\bar{g}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\bar{\rho}}
\scriptstyle{\delta^{k}\mathbin{\hat{\otimes}}\sigma}
\textstyle{\bar{g}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sigma}
\textstyle{g\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\theta^{k}\mathbin{\hat{\otimes}}g}
\textstyle{\delta^{k}\mathbin{\hat{\otimes}}g\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rho}
\textstyle{g}

in
\cal{E}^{\to}
commutes, where
\sigma\colon\bar{g}\to g
is the pullback in (
3.24
) and the horizontal composites should be identities.
Since
\sigma
is a cartesian arrow with respect to the codomain fibration, it suffices to solve this problem on codomains.
Again omitting horizontal composites, we need a dotted arrow in

\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\delta^{1-k}\otimes X}
\scriptstyle{p}
\textstyle{I\otimes X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{cod}(\bar{\rho})}
\scriptstyle{I\otimes p}
\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p}
\textstyle{Y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\delta^{1-k}\otimes Y}
\textstyle{I\otimes Y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{cod}(\rho)}
\textstyle{Y\hbox to0.0pt{.\hss}}

This is equivalent to finding a diagonal filler in the following square:

\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\delta^{1-k}\otimes X}
\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p}
\textstyle{I\otimes X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{I\otimes p}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{cod}(\bar{\rho})}
\textstyle{I\otimes Y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{cod}(\rho)}
\textstyle{Y\hbox to0.0pt{.\hss}}

But
\delta^{1-k}\otimes X\cong\delta^{1-k}\mathbin{\hat{\otimes}}\bot_{X}
, and
\bot_{X}\colon 0\to X
is a cofibration by the assumption that
(\mathsf{Cof},\mathsf{TrivFib})
is suitable (condition (S2) of
definition

2.1
).
By
corollary

2.9
, we have that
\delta^{1-k}\mathbin{\hat{\otimes}}\bot_{X}\in\mathsf{TrivCof}
, and hence have a diagonal filler since
p\in\mathsf{Fib}
.
∎

Theorem 3.8
.

The wfs
(\mathsf{TrivCof},\mathsf{Fib})
has the Frobenius property.

By
proposition

1.4
, it suffices to show that, for a pullback of the form

\begin{gathered}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 9.71356pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-6.75pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-9.71356pt\raise-15.66666pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.63542pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{j}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-24.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 31.53471pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 10.18231pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-10.18231pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\lower 0.0pt\hbox{\lower 0.0pt\hbox{\line@@}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\lower 0.0pt\hbox{\lower-5.0pt\hbox{\line@@}}}\hbox{}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 31.53471pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{B\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 38.57811pt\raise-15.66666pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.31596pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{i}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 38.57811pt\raise-24.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-7.53471pt\raise-31.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.22787pt\raise-36.5208pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.8264pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{p}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 31.56422pt\raise-31.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 31.56422pt\raise-31.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{Y\hbox to0.0pt{,\hss}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{gathered}

where
i\in\cal{I}_{\otimes}
and
p\in\mathsf{Fib}
, we have
j\in\mathsf{TrivCof}
.
By part (i) of
lemma

3.5
,
i\in\mathsf{Cof}\cap S
.
By the closure of
\mathsf{Cof}
under pullback (condition (S3) of
definition

2.1
) and
lemma

3.7
, it follows that
j\in\mathsf{Cof}\cap S
.
But
\mathsf{Cof}\cap S\subseteq\mathsf{TrivCof}
by part (ii) of
lemma

3.5
.
∎

Example 3.9
.

When applied to the category of simplicial sets (see
example

2.6
),
theorem

3.8
gives a new proof of right properness of the Quillen model structure for Kan complexes.
This proof avoids the use of topological realization, which is used in
[
21
, Theorem 13.1.13]
to deduce the desired result from the right properness of the model structure on topological spaces in which the fibrations are the Serre fibrations.
It also avoids the use of the theory of minimal fibrations, which is used in
[
26
, Theorem 1.7.1]
to establish the result working purely combinatorially.

4.
Categories of orthogonal maps

This section starts the second part of the paper, in which we are interested in generalizations of the results obtained earlier to algebraic weak factorization systems (awf’s)
[
16
,
20
]
.
In this section and the next, we will need to review and establish some facts about the algebraic setting that are useful for our development.
Importantly, in the algebraic setting we do not consider just classes of arrows in
\cal{E}
, but rather categories
\cal{I}
, to be thought of as indexing categories, and functors
u\colon\cal{I}\to\cal{E}^{\to}
.
In the following, we write
u_{i}\colon A_{i}\to B_{i}
for the result of applying such a functor to
i\in\cal{I}
.
Let us recall the following definition from
[
16
]
.

Definition 4.1
.

Let
u\colon\cal{I}\to\cal{E}^{\to}
.

(i)

A
right
\cal{I}
-map

(f,\phi)\colon X\to Y
consists of a map
f\colon X\to Y
in
\cal{E}
and a function
\phi
that assigns to each
i\in\cal{I}
and commuting square

\textstyle{A_{i}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{s}
\scriptstyle{u_{i}}
\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{B_{i}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{t}
\textstyle{Y\hbox to0.0pt{,\hss}}

a diagonal filler
\phi(i,s,t)\colon B_{i}\to X
, satisfying the following naturality condition: for every diagram of the form

\textstyle{A_{i}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{a}
\scriptstyle{u_{i}}
\textstyle{A_{j}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{s}
\scriptstyle{u_{j}}
\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{B_{i}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{b}
\textstyle{B_{j}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{t}
\textstyle{Y}

where the left square is the image of
\sigma\colon i\to j
in
\cal{I}
under
u
, we have that

\phi(j,s,t)\mathbin{\circ}b=\phi(i,s\mathbin{\circ}a,t\mathbin{\circ}b)\,.

(ii)

A
right
\cal{I}
-map morphism

\alpha\colon(f,\phi)\to(f^{\prime},\phi^{\prime})
is a square
\alpha\colon f\to f^{\prime}
in
\cal{E}
satisfying an evident compatibility condition with respect to the choices of diagonal fillers.

For
u\colon\cal{I}\to\cal{E}^{\to}
, we write
{\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\smash{\cal{I}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-10.89029pt\hskip 10.89029pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-10.89029pt\hskip 10.89029pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\smash{\cal{I}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-10.89029pt\hskip 10.89029pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-10.89029pt\hskip 10.89029pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\smash{\cal{I}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-9.051pt\hskip 9.051pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-9.051pt\hskip 9.051pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\smash{\cal{I}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-8.19528pt\hskip 8.19528pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-8.19528pt\hskip 8.19528pt}}}}
for the category of right
\cal{I}
-maps and their morphisms.
We then let
{\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\smash{u}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-10.90512pt\hskip 10.90512pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-10.90512pt\hskip 10.90512pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\smash{u}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-10.90512pt\hskip 10.90512pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-10.90512pt\hskip 10.90512pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\smash{u}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-8.81627pt\hskip 8.81627pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-8.81627pt\hskip 8.81627pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\smash{u}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-7.74777pt\hskip 7.74777pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-7.74777pt\hskip 7.74777pt}}}}\colon{\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\smash{\cal{I}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-10.89029pt\hskip 10.89029pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-10.89029pt\hskip 10.89029pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\smash{\cal{I}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-10.89029pt\hskip 10.89029pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-10.89029pt\hskip 10.89029pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\smash{\cal{I}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-9.051pt\hskip 9.051pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-9.051pt\hskip 9.051pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\smash{\cal{I}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-8.19528pt\hskip 8.19528pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-8.19528pt\hskip 8.19528pt}}}}\to\cal{E}^{\to}
be the evident forgetful functor, which we call the
right orthogonal
of
u
.
Analogously, we can define the category
{\mathchoice{{\hskip 5.3889pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\smash{\cal{I}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip-5.3889pt\hskip-10.89029pt\pitchfork\hskip 10.89029pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-10.89029pt\hskip 10.89029pt}}}{{\hskip 5.3889pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\smash{\cal{I}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip-5.3889pt\hskip-10.89029pt\pitchfork\hskip 10.89029pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-10.89029pt\hskip 10.89029pt}}}{{\hskip 4.72229pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\smash{\cal{I}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-4.72229pt\hskip-9.051pt\pitchfork\hskip 9.051pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-9.051pt\hskip 9.051pt}}}{{\hskip 4.72229pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\smash{\cal{I}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-4.72229pt\hskip-8.19528pt\pitchfork\hskip 8.19528pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-8.19528pt\hskip 8.19528pt}}}}
of left
\cal{I}
-maps and their morphisms as well as the forgetful functor
{\mathchoice{{\hskip 5.3889pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\smash{u}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip-5.3889pt\hskip-10.90512pt\pitchfork\hskip 10.90512pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-10.90512pt\hskip 10.90512pt}}}{{\hskip 5.3889pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\smash{u}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip-5.3889pt\hskip-10.90512pt\pitchfork\hskip 10.90512pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-10.90512pt\hskip 10.90512pt}}}{{\hskip 4.72229pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\smash{u}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-4.72229pt\hskip-8.81627pt\pitchfork\hskip 8.81627pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-8.81627pt\hskip 8.81627pt}}}{{\hskip 4.72229pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\smash{u}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-4.72229pt\hskip-7.74777pt\pitchfork\hskip 7.74777pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-7.74777pt\hskip 7.74777pt}}}}\colon{\mathchoice{{\hskip 5.3889pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\smash{\cal{I}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip-5.3889pt\hskip-10.89029pt\pitchfork\hskip 10.89029pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-10.89029pt\hskip 10.89029pt}}}{{\hskip 5.3889pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\smash{\cal{I}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip-5.3889pt\hskip-10.89029pt\pitchfork\hskip 10.89029pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-10.89029pt\hskip 10.89029pt}}}{{\hskip 4.72229pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\smash{\cal{I}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-4.72229pt\hskip-9.051pt\pitchfork\hskip 9.051pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-9.051pt\hskip 9.051pt}}}{{\hskip 4.72229pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\smash{\cal{I}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-4.72229pt\hskip-8.19528pt\pitchfork\hskip 8.19528pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-8.19528pt\hskip 8.19528pt}}}}\to\cal{E}^{\to}
, which we call the
left orthogonal
of
u
.
As shown in
[
16
, Proposition 3.8]
, the orthogonality operations
extend to functors forming an adjunction

(4.1)

\begin{gathered}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 21.51039pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-21.51039pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathbf{CAT}_{/\cal{E}^{\to}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 9.52403pt\raise 12.79501pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-3.04501pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{{\mathchoice{{\hskip Even if objects of
\mathbf{CAT}_{/\cal{E}^{\to}}
are pairs of the form
(\cal{I},u)
where
\cal{I}
is a category
u\colon\cal{I}\to\cal{E}^{\to}
and is a functor, in the following we shall often refer to them by the domain
\cal{I}
of
u
.
A similar convention applies to maps in
\mathbf{CAT}_{/\cal{E}^{\to}}
.
For example, we write the components of the unit and counit simply as

\eta{I}\colon\cal{I}\to{\mathchoice{{\hskip 5.3889pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\smash{({\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\smash{\cal{I}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 8.19528pt}}}})}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip-5.3889pt\hskip-74.20758pt\pitchfork\hskip 74.20758pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-74.20758pt\hskip 74.20758pt}}}{{\hskip 3.26666pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\smash{\cal{I}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip-3.26666pt\hskip-9.051pt\pitchfork\hskip 9.051pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-9.051pt\hskip 9.051pt}}}{{\hskip 2.33333pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\smash{\cal{I}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-2.33333pt\hskip-5.33624pt\pitchfork\hskip 5.33624pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.33624pt\hskip 5.33624pt}}}{{\hskip 2.33333pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\smash{\cal{I}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-2.33333pt\hskip-3.81158pt\pitchfork\hskip 3.81158pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 2.33333pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\smash{\cal{I}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip-2.33333pt\hskip-8.19528pt\pitchfork\hskip 8.19528pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-8.19528pt\hskip 8.19528pt}}}{{\hskip 2.33333pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\smash{\cal{I}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip-2.33333pt\hskip-8.19528pt\pitchfork\hskip 8.19528pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-8.19528pt\hskip 8.19528pt}}}{{\hskip 1.66667pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\smash{\cal{I}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-1.66667pt\hskip-3.81158pt\pitchfork\hskip 3.81158pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-3.81158pt\hskip 3.81158pt}}}{{\hskip 1.66667pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\smash{\cal{I}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-1.66667pt\hskip-2.72256pt\pitchfork\hskip 2.72256pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-2.72256pt\hskip 2.72256pt}}}})}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-54.08119pt\hskip 54.08119pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-54.08119pt\hskip 54.08119pt}}}}\,.

In the following, we will see that some familiar equalities between two classes of maps are replaced by functors back and forth between two categories over
\cal{E}^{\to}
, for which we now introduce some notation.
Given
u\colon\cal{I}\to\cal{E}^{\to}
and
v\colon\cal{J}\to\cal{E}^{\to}
, we write

(4.2)

\cal{I}\leftrightarrow\cal{J}

to mean that there are functors
F\colon\cal{I}\to\cal{J}
and
G\colon\cal{J}\to\cal{I}
over
\cal{E}^{\to}
, not necessarily forming an isomorphism or even an equivalence.

The rest of this section is devoted to establishing some facts regarding categories of orthogonal maps, describing the interplay between orthogonality functors and retract closure, slicing, adjunctions, Leibniz adjunctions, and Kan extensions.
These facts are expected generalizations of well-known statements for classes of weakly orthogonal classes in the standard setting.
Some of them follow from results in
[
7
]
; the others are probably also known to experts, but we include them since they are used in the remainder of the paper and we could not find them in the literature.
We omit most proofs, which are straightforward.

Given a functor
u\colon\cal{I}\to\cal{E}^{\to}
, we define its
retract closure

\overline{u}\colon\overline{\cal{I}}\to\cal{E}^{\to}
as follows.
An object of
\overline{\cal{I}}
is a tuple
(i,e,\sigma,\rho)
consisting of an object
i\in\cal{I}
, an object
e\in\cal{E}^{\to}
, and maps
\sigma\colon e\rightarrow u_{i}
,
\rho\colon u_{i}\rightarrow e
in
\cal{E}^{\to}
which exhibit
e
as a retract of
u_{i}
in
\cal{E}^{\to}
,
i.e.
such that

\textstyle{e\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sigma}
\textstyle{u_{i}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rho}
\textstyle{e\hbox to0.0pt{.\hss}}

A map
(f,\kappa)\colon(i,e,\sigma,\rho)\to(i^{\prime},e^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime},\rho^{\prime})
in
\overline{\cal{I}}
consists of a map
f\colon i\to i^{\prime}
in
\cal{I}
and a map
\kappa\colon e\rightarrow e^{\prime}
in
\cal{E}^{\to}
such that the following diagram in
\cal{E}^{\to}
commutes:

\textstyle{e\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sigma}
\scriptstyle{\kappa}
\textstyle{u_{i}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rho}
\scriptstyle{u_{f}}
\textstyle{e\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\kappa}
\textstyle{e^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sigma^{\prime}}
\textstyle{u_{i^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rho^{\prime}}
\textstyle{e^{\prime}\hbox to0.0pt{.\hss}}

The functor
\overline{u}\colon\overline{\cal{I}}\to\cal{E}^{\to}
is then defined on objects by letting
\overline{u}(i,e,\sigma,\rho)=_{\operatorname{def}}e
, and on maps by letting
\overline{u}(f,\kappa)=_{\operatorname{def}}\kappa
.
The operation of retract closure gives a monad in an evident way.
The next proposition uses the notation introduced in (
4.2
).

Proposition 4.2
.

For every
u\colon\cal{I}\to\cal{E}^{\to}
, we have pairs of functors

{\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\smash{\left\lparen\overline{\cal{I}}\right\rparen}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-17.95836pt\hskip 17.95836pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-17.95836pt\hskip 17.95836pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\smash{\left\lparen\overline{\cal{I}}\right\rparen}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-17.95836pt\hskip 17.95836pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-17.95836pt\hskip 17.95836pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\smash{\left\lparen\overline{\cal{I}}\right\rparen}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-16.86603pt\hskip 16.86603pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-16.86603pt\hskip 16.86603pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\smash{\left\lparen\overline{\cal{I}}\right\rparen}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-16.2952pt\hskip 16.2952pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-16.2952pt\hskip 16.2952pt}}}}\leftrightarrow{\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\smash{\cal{I}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-10.89029pt\hskip 10.89029pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-10.89029pt\hskip 10.89029pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\smash{\cal{I}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-10.89029pt\hskip 10.89029pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-10.89029pt\hskip 10.89029pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\smash{\cal{I}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-9.051pt\hskip 9.051pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-9.051pt\hskip 9.051pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\smash{\cal{I}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-8.19528pt\hskip 8.19528pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-8.19528pt\hskip 8.19528pt}}}}\,,\quad{\mathchoice{{\hskip 5.3889pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\smash{\left\lparen\overline{\cal{I}}\right\rparen}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip-5.3889pt\hskip-17.95836pt\pitchfork\hskip 17.95836pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-17.95836pt\hskip 17.95836pt}}}{{\hskip 5.3889pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\smash{\left\lparen\overline{\cal{I}}\right\rparen}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip-5.3889pt\hskip-17.95836pt\pitchfork\hskip 17.95836pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-17.95836pt\hskip 17.95836pt}}}{{\hskip 4.72229pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\smash{\left\lparen\overline{\cal{I}}\right\rparen}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-4.72229pt\hskip-16.86603pt\pitchfork\hskip 16.86603pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-16.86603pt\hskip 16.86603pt}}}{{\hskip 4.72229pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\smash{\left\lparen\overline{\cal{I}}\right\rparen}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-4.72229pt\hskip-16.2952pt\pitchfork\hskip 16.2952pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-16.2952pt\hskip 16.2952pt}}}}\leftrightarrow{\mathchoice{{\hskip 5.3889pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\smash{\cal{I}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip-5.3889pt\hskip-10.89029pt\pitchfork\hskip 10.89029pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-10.89029pt\hskip 10.89029pt}}}{{\hskip 5.3889pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\smash{\cal{I}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip-5.3889pt\hskip-10.89029pt\pitchfork\hskip 10.89029pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-10.89029pt\hskip 10.89029pt}}}{{\hskip 4.72229pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\smash{\cal{I}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-4.72229pt\hskip-9.051pt\pitchfork\hskip 9.051pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-9.051pt\hskip 9.051pt}}}{{\hskip 4.72229pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\smash{\cal{I}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-4.72229pt\hskip-8.19528pt\pitchfork\hskip 8.19528pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-8.19528pt\hskip 8.19528pt}}}}

over
\cal{E}^{\to}
.
∎

Given
u\colon\cal{I}\to\cal{E}^{\to}
and
X\in\cal{E}
, we define the
slice category

\cal{I}_{/X}
and functor
u_{/X}\colon\cal{I}_{/X}\to\cal{E}_{/X}^{\to}
as follows.
The category
\cal{I}_{/X}
has as objects pairs consisting of an object
i\in\cal{I}
and a commutative triangle in
\cal{E}
of the form

\textstyle{A_{i}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{u_{i}}
\textstyle{B_{i}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{X\hbox to0.0pt{.\hss}}

The functor
u_{/X}\colon\cal{I}_{/X}\to\cal{E}_{/X}^{\to}
sends such a pair to
u_{i}\colon A_{i}\to B_{i}
, viewed as a morphism in
\cal{E}_{/X}
.
There is also a
coslice
category under
X
, described dually, which we denote
u_{\backslash X}\colon\cal{I}_{\backslash X}\to\cal{E}^{\to}
.
With these definitions in place, the commutation between slicing and orthogonality functors in the algebraic setting can be stated as follows.

Proposition 4.3
.

Let
u\colon\cal{I}\to\cal{E}^{\to}
and
X\in\cal{E}
.

(i)

The right orthogonality functor commutes with slicing,
i.e.
we have
{\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\smash{(\cal{I}_{/X})}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-28.8862pt\hskip 28.8862pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-28.8862pt\hskip 28.8862pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\smash{(\cal{I}_{/X})}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-28.8862pt\hskip 28.8862pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-28.8862pt\hskip 28.8862pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\smash{(\cal{I}_{/X})}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-24.18706pt\hskip 24.18706pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-24.18706pt\hskip 24.18706pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\smash{(\cal{I}_{/X})}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-22.49803pt\hskip 22.49803pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-22.49803pt\hskip 22.49803pt}}}}=({\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\smash{\cal{I}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-10.89029pt\hskip 10.89029pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-10.89029pt\hskip 10.89029pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\smash{\cal{I}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-10.89029pt\hskip 10.89029pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-10.89029pt\hskip 10.89029pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\smash{\cal{I}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-9.051pt\hskip 9.051pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-9.051pt\hskip 9.051pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\smash{\cal{I}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-8.19528pt\hskip 8.19528pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-8.19528pt\hskip 8.19528pt}}}})_{/X}
as categories over
\cal{E}^{\to}
.

(ii)

The left orthogonality functor commutes with coslicing,
i.e.
we have
{\mathchoice{{\hskip 5.3889pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\smash{(\cal{I}_{\backslash X})}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip-5.3889pt\hskip-28.9973pt\pitchfork\hskip 28.9973pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-28.9973pt\hskip 28.9973pt}}}{{\hskip 5.3889pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\smash{(\cal{I}_{\backslash X})}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip-5.3889pt\hskip-28.9973pt\pitchfork\hskip 28.9973pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-28.9973pt\hskip 28.9973pt}}}{{\hskip 4.72229pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\smash{(\cal{I}_{\backslash X})}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-4.72229pt\hskip-24.46481pt\pitchfork\hskip 24.46481pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-24.46481pt\hskip 24.46481pt}}}{{\hskip 4.72229pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\smash{(\cal{I}_{\backslash X})}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-4.72229pt\hskip-22.77579pt\pitchfork\hskip 22.77579pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-22.77579pt\hskip 22.77579pt}}}}=({\mathchoice{{\hskip 5.3889pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\smash{\cal{I}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip-5.3889pt\hskip-10.89029pt\pitchfork\hskip 10.89029pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-10.89029pt\hskip 10.89029pt}}}{{\hskip 5.3889pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\smash{\cal{I}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip-5.3889pt\hskip-10.89029pt\pitchfork\hskip 10.89029pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-10.89029pt\hskip 10.89029pt}}}{{\hskip 4.72229pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\smash{\cal{I}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-4.72229pt\hskip-9.051pt\pitchfork\hskip 9.051pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-9.051pt\hskip 9.051pt}}}{{\hskip 4.72229pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\smash{\cal{I}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-4.72229pt\hskip-8.19528pt\pitchfork\hskip 8.19528pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-8.19528pt\hskip 8.19528pt}}}})_{\backslash X}
as categories over
\cal{E}^{\to}
.
∎

In contrast to the right orthogonality functor, the left orthogonality functor does not commute with slicing in general.
However, it does under certain assumptions, as described in
proposition

4.4
below.

Proposition 4.4
.

Let
u\colon\cal{I}\to\cal{E}^{\to}
and assume that

\textstyle{\cal{I}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{u}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{cod}_{\cal{E}}{}\mathbin{\circ}{}u}
\textstyle{\cal{E}^{\to}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{cod}_{\cal{E}}}
\textstyle{\cal{E}}

is a morphism of Grothendieck fibrations.
Then the left orthogonality functor commutes with slicing on
\cal{I}
,
i.e.
for
X\in\cal{E}
we have
{\mathchoice{{\hskip 5.3889pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\smash{(\cal{I}_{/X})}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip-5.3889pt\hskip-28.8862pt\pitchfork\hskip 28.8862pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-28.8862pt\hskip 28.8862pt}}}{{\hskip 5.3889pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\smash{(\cal{I}_{/X})}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip-5.3889pt\hskip-28.8862pt\pitchfork\hskip 28.8862pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-28.8862pt\hskip 28.8862pt}}}{{\hskip 4.72229pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\smash{(\cal{I}_{/X})}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-4.72229pt\hskip-24.18706pt\pitchfork\hskip 24.18706pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-24.18706pt\hskip 24.18706pt}}}{{\hskip 4.72229pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\smash{(\cal{I}_{/X})}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-4.72229pt\hskip-22.49803pt\pitchfork\hskip 22.49803pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-22.49803pt\hskip 22.49803pt}}}}=({\mathchoice{{\hskip 5.3889pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\smash{\cal{I}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip-5.3889pt\hskip-10.89029pt\pitchfork\hskip 10.89029pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-10.89029pt\hskip 10.89029pt}}}{{\hskip 5.3889pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\smash{\cal{I}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip-5.3889pt\hskip-10.89029pt\pitchfork\hskip 10.89029pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-10.89029pt\hskip 10.89029pt}}}{{\hskip 4.72229pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\smash{\cal{I}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-4.72229pt\hskip-9.051pt\pitchfork\hskip 9.051pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-9.051pt\hskip 9.051pt}}}{{\hskip 4.72229pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\smash{\cal{I}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-4.72229pt\hskip-8.19528pt\pitchfork\hskip 8.19528pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-8.19528pt\hskip 8.19528pt}}}})_{/X}
.

First note that the composite
\operatorname{cod}_{\cal{E}_{/X}}{}\mathrel{\mathbin{\circ}}{}u_{/X}
is also a Grothendieck fibration.
When constructing the category of left maps for
\cal{I}
or
\cal{I}_{/X}
, an application of base change and naturality of diagonal fillers shows that it is sufficient to consider lifting problems with the bottom arrow an identity.
But this restricted left orthogonality functor evidently commutes with slicing.
∎

.

(i)

Use
proposition

4.3
and note, for the first statement, that categories of right maps satisfy the assumptions of
proposition

4.4
.
The second statement follows dually.
∎

Proposition 4.6
.

The retract closure commutes with slicing and coslicing, in the sense that for every
u\colon\cal{I}\to\cal{E}^{\to}
, we have
\overline{\cal{I}_{/X}}=\overline{\cal{I}}_{/X}
and
\overline{\cal{I}_{\backslash X}}=\overline{\cal{I}}_{\backslash X}
as categories over
\cal{E}^{\to}
.
∎

propositions

4.7
and
4.8
below are implied by
[
7
, Proposition 21]
with double categories of squares specialized to categories of arrows.

proposition

4.7
appears also in the form of the pullback square (6.3) in
[
35
]
.

Proposition 4.7
.

Let
u\colon\cal{I}\to\cal{E}^{\to}
and
v\colon\cal{J}\to\cal{F}^{\to}
.
For an adjunction
F\dashv G
between
\cal{E}
and
\cal{F}
, the following are equivalent, naturally in
\cal{I}
and
\cal{J}
:

(i)

the functor
F\colon\cal{E}^{\to}\to\cal{F}^{\to}
lifts to a functor
\widetilde{F}\colon\cal{I}\to{\mathchoice{{\hskip 5.3889pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\smash{\cal{J}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip-5.3889pt\hskip-12.22295pt\pitchfork\hskip 12.22295pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-12.22295pt\hskip 12.22295pt}}}{{\hskip 5.3889pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\smash{\cal{J}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip-5.3889pt\hskip-12.22295pt\pitchfork\hskip 12.22295pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-12.22295pt\hskip 12.22295pt}}}{{\hskip 4.72229pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\smash{\cal{J}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-4.72229pt\hskip-10.00053pt\pitchfork\hskip 10.00053pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-10.00053pt\hskip 10.00053pt}}}{{\hskip 4.72229pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\smash{\cal{J}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-4.72229pt\hskip-8.88936pt\pitchfork\hskip 8.88936pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-8.88936pt\hskip 8.88936pt}}}}
making the following diagram commute:

\textstyle{\cal{I}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{F}}
\scriptstyle{u}
\textstyle{{\mathchoice{{\hskip 5.3889pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\smash{\cal{J}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip-5.3889pt\hskip-12.22295pt\pitchfork\hskip 12.22295pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-12.22295pt\hskip 12.22295pt}}}{{\hskip 5.3889pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\smash{\cal{J}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip-5.3889pt\hskip-12.22295pt\pitchfork\hskip 12.22295pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-12.22295pt\hskip 12.22295pt}}}{{\hskip 4.72229pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\smash{\cal{J}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-4.72229pt\hskip-10.00053pt\pitchfork\hskip 10.00053pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-10.00053pt\hskip 10.00053pt}}}{{\hskip 4.72229pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\smash{\cal{J}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-4.72229pt\hskip-8.88936pt\pitchfork\hskip 8.88936pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-8.88936pt\hskip 8.88936pt}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\mathchoice{{\hskip 3.26666pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\smash{v}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip-3.26666pt\hskip-8.3394pt\pitchfork\hskip 8.3394pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-8.3394pt\hskip 8.3394pt}}}{{\hskip 3.26666pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\smash{v}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip-3.26666pt\hskip-8.3394pt\pitchfork\hskip 8.3394pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-8.3394pt\hskip 8.3394pt}}}{{\hskip 2.33333pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\smash{v}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-2.33333pt\hskip-5.0894pt\pitchfork\hskip 5.0894pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.0894pt\hskip 5.0894pt}}}{{\hskip 2.33333pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\smash{v}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-2.33333pt\hskip-3.63528pt\pitchfork\hskip 3.63528pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-3.63528pt\hskip 3.63528pt}}}}}
\textstyle{\cal{E}^{\to}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{F}
\textstyle{\cal{F}^{\to}\hbox to0.0pt{,\hss}}

(ii)

the functor
G\colon\cal{F}^{\to}\to\cal{E}^{\to}
lifts to a functor
\widetilde{G}\colon\cal{J}\to{\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\smash{\cal{I}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-10.89029pt\hskip 10.89029pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-10.89029pt\hskip 10.89029pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\smash{\cal{I}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-10.89029pt\hskip 10.89029pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-10.89029pt\hskip 10.89029pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\smash{\cal{I}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-9.051pt\hskip 9.051pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-9.051pt\hskip 9.051pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\smash{\cal{I}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-8.19528pt\hskip 8.19528pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-8.19528pt\hskip 8.19528pt}}}}
making the following diagram commute:

\textstyle{\cal{J}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{v}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{G}}
\textstyle{{\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\smash{\cal{I}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-10.89029pt\hskip 10.89029pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-10.89029pt\hskip 10.89029pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\smash{\cal{I}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-10.89029pt\hskip 10.89029pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-10.89029pt\hskip 10.89029pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\smash{\cal{I}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-9.051pt\hskip 9.051pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-9.051pt\hskip 9.051pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\smash{\cal{I}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-8.19528pt\hskip 8.19528pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-8.19528pt\hskip 8.19528pt}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\smash{u}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-8.81627pt\hskip 8.81627pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-8.81627pt\hskip 8.81627pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\smash{u}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-8.81627pt\hskip 8.81627pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-8.81627pt\hskip 8.81627pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\smash{u}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.3435pt\hskip 5.3435pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.3435pt\hskip 5.3435pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\smash{u}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-3.81679pt\hskip 3.81679pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-3.81679pt\hskip 3.81679pt}}}}}
\textstyle{\cal{F}^{\to}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{G}
\textstyle{\cal{E}^{\to}\hbox to0.0pt{.\hss}}

∎

Proposition 4.8
.

Let
u\colon\cal{I}\to\cal{E}^{\to}
and
v\colon\cal{J}\to\cal{F}^{\to}
.
Let
F_{1}\dashv G_{1}
and
F_{2}\dashv G_{2}
be adjunctions between
\cal{E}
and
\cal{F}
satisfying the equivalent conditions of
proposition

4.7
.
Let
m\colon F_{1}\to F_{2}
and
n\colon G_{2}\to G_{1}
be natural transformations forming mates.
Then the following are equivalent:

(i)

The natural transformation
m
lifts as follows:

\textstyle{\cal{I}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\scriptstyle\widetilde{F_{1}}}
\textstyle{\scriptstyle\widetilde{F_{2}}}
\textstyle{\scriptstyle\widetilde{m}}
\scriptstyle{u}
\textstyle{{\mathchoice{{\hskip 5.3889pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\smash{\cal{J}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip-5.3889pt\hskip-12.22295pt\pitchfork\hskip 12.22295pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-12.22295pt\hskip 12.22295pt}}}{{\hskip 5.3889pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\smash{\cal{J}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip-5.3889pt\hskip-12.22295pt\pitchfork\hskip 12.22295pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-12.22295pt\hskip 12.22295pt}}}{{\hskip 4.72229pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\smash{\cal{J}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-4.72229pt\hskip-10.00053pt\pitchfork\hskip 10.00053pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-10.00053pt\hskip 10.00053pt}}}{{\hskip 4.72229pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\smash{\cal{J}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-4.72229pt\hskip-8.88936pt\pitchfork\hskip 8.88936pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-8.88936pt\hskip 8.88936pt}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\mathchoice{{\hskip 3.26666pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\smash{v}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip-3.26666pt\hskip-8.3394pt\pitchfork\hskip 8.3394pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-8.3394pt\hskip 8.3394pt}}}{{\hskip 3.26666pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\smash{v}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip-3.26666pt\hskip-8.3394pt\pitchfork\hskip 8.3394pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-8.3394pt\hskip 8.3394pt}}}{{\hskip 2.33333pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\smash{v}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-2.33333pt\hskip-5.0894pt\pitchfork\hskip 5.0894pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.0894pt\hskip 5.0894pt}}}{{\hskip 2.33333pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\smash{v}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-2.33333pt\hskip-3.63528pt\pitchfork\hskip 3.63528pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-3.63528pt\hskip 3.63528pt}}}}}
\textstyle{\cal{E}^{\to}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\scriptstyle F_{1}}
\textstyle{\scriptstyle F_{2}}
\textstyle{\scriptstyle m}
\textstyle{\cal{F}^{\to}\hbox to0.0pt{,\hss}}

(ii)

The natural transformation

lifts as follows:

\textstyle{\cal{J}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

\scriptstyle{{\mathchoice{{\hskip 3.26666pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\smash{v}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip-3.26666pt\hskip-8.3394pt\pitchfork\hskip 8.3394pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-8.3394pt\hskip 8.3394pt}}}{{\hskip 3.26666pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\smash{v}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip-3.26666pt\hskip-8.3394pt\pitchfork\hskip 8.3394pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-8.3394pt\hskip 8.3394pt}}}{{\hskip 2.33333pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\smash{v}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-2.33333pt\hskip-5.0894pt\pitchfork\hskip 5.0894pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.0894pt\hskip 5.0894pt}}}{{\hskip 2.33333pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\smash{v}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-2.33333pt\hskip-3.63528pt\pitchfork\hskip 3.63528pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-3.63528pt\hskip 3.63528pt}}}}}
\textstyle{\cal{F}^{\to}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\scriptstyle G_{2}}
\textstyle{\scriptstyle G_{1}}
\textstyle{\scriptstyle n}
\textstyle{\cal{E}^{\to}\hbox to0.0pt{.\hss}}

We generalize
proposition

4.7
to Leibniz adjunctions
[
36
]
.
Let us fix functors
F\colon\cal{K}\times\cal{E}\to\cal{F}
and
G\colon\cal{K}^{\operatorname{op}}\times\cal{F}\to\cal{E}
related, for
k\in\cal{K}
, by an adjunction:

\textstyle{\cal{E}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{F(k,-)}
\scriptstyle{\bot}
\textstyle{\cal{F}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox to0.0pt{.\hss}}
\scriptstyle{G(k,-)}

Let
\widehat{F}\colon\cal{K}^{\to}\times\cal{E}^{\to}\to\cal{F}^{\to}
and
\widehat{G}\colon(\cal{K}^{\operatorname{op}})^{\to}\times\cal{F}^{\to}\to\cal{E}^{\to}
denote the Leibniz constructions for
F
and
G^{\operatorname{op}}
, using pullback instead of pushout for
\widehat{G}
.
Here and below we assume that the categories under consideration have sufficient structure to carry out the relevant Leibniz constructions.
The following proposition is a generalization of
[
35
, Theorem 6.5]
.

Corollary 4.10
.

For a functor
u\colon\cal{I}\to\cal{E}^{\to}
and a map
h\colon X\to Y
in
\cal{K}
, there is a pullback of categories of the form

\textstyle{{\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\smash{\cal{I}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-10.89029pt\hskip 10.89029pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-10.89029pt\hskip 10.89029pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\smash{\cal{I}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-10.89029pt\hskip 10.89029pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-10.89029pt\hskip 10.89029pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\smash{\cal{I}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-9.051pt\hskip 9.051pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-9.051pt\hskip 9.051pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\smash{\cal{I}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-8.19528pt\hskip 8.19528pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-8.19528pt\hskip 8.19528pt}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\smash{(\widehat{F}(h,-)\mathbin{\circ}u)}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-40.60924pt\hskip 40.60924pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-40.60924pt\hskip 40.60924pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\smash{(\widehat{F}(h,-)\mathbin{\circ}u)}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-40.60924pt\hskip 40.60924pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-40.60924pt\hskip 40.60924pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\smash{(\widehat{F}(h,-)\mathbin{\circ}u)}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-26.7889pt\hskip 26.7889pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-26.7889pt\hskip 26.7889pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\smash{(\widehat{F}(h,-)\mathbin{\circ}u)}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-20.72224pt\hskip 20.72224pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-20.72224pt\hskip 20.72224pt}}}}}
\textstyle{{\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\smash{\cal{I}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-10.89029pt\hskip 10.89029pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-10.89029pt\hskip 10.89029pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\smash{\cal{I}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-10.89029pt\hskip 10.89029pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-10.89029pt\hskip 10.89029pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\smash{\cal{I}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-9.051pt\hskip 9.051pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-9.051pt\hskip 9.051pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\smash{\cal{I}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-8.19528pt\hskip 8.19528pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-8.19528pt\hskip 8.19528pt}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\smash{u}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-8.81627pt\hskip 8.81627pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-8.81627pt\hskip 8.81627pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\smash{u}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-8.81627pt\hskip 8.81627pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-8.81627pt\hskip 8.81627pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\smash{u}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.3435pt\hskip 5.3435pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.3435pt\hskip 5.3435pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\smash{u}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-3.81679pt\hskip 3.81679pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-3.81679pt\hskip 3.81679pt}}}}}
\textstyle{\cal{F}^{\to}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widehat{G}(h,-)}
\textstyle{\cal{E}^{\to}\hbox to0.0pt{.\hss}}

By the Yoneda lemma and the universal property of pullbacks, it suffices to show that diagrams of the form

\textstyle{\cal{J}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{v}
\textstyle{{\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\smash{\cal{I}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-10.89029pt\hskip 10.89029pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-10.89029pt\hskip 10.89029pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\smash{\cal{I}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-10.89029pt\hskip 10.89029pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-10.89029pt\hskip 10.89029pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\smash{\cal{I}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-9.051pt\hskip 9.051pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-9.051pt\hskip 9.051pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\smash{\cal{I}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-8.19528pt\hskip 8.19528pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-8.19528pt\hskip 8.19528pt}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\smash{(\widehat{F}(h,-)\mathbin{\circ}u)}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-40.60924pt\hskip 40.60924pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-40.60924pt\hskip 40.60924pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\smash{(\widehat{F}(h,-)\mathbin{\circ}u)}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-40.60924pt\hskip 40.60924pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-40.60924pt\hskip 40.60924pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\smash{(\widehat{F}(h,-)\mathbin{\circ}u)}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-26.7889pt\hskip 26.7889pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-26.7889pt\hskip 26.7889pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\smash{(\widehat{F}(h,-)\mathbin{\circ}u)}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-20.72224pt\hskip 20.72224pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-20.72224pt\hskip 20.72224pt}}}}}
\textstyle{\cal{F}^{\to}\hbox to0.0pt{.\hss}}

are in natural bijective correspondence with diagrams as in item (ii) of
proposition

4.9
.
But by the adjunction (
4.1
), the former are in natural bijective correspondence with diagrams as in item (i) of
proposition

4.9
.
∎

The next results spell out a special case of
propositions

4.9
and
4.10
which will be used in
sections

6
and
7
.
Fix categories
\cal{E}
and
\cal{F}
, and let
\operatorname{Adj}(\cal{E},\cal{F})
be the category of adjunctions
U\dashv V
(with
U\colon\cal{E}\to\cal{F}
and
V\colon\cal{F}\to\cal{E}
) and maps of adjunctions
[
30
, Chapter IV,§7]
.
We then have functors

\operatorname{ev}_{L}\colon\operatorname{Adj}(\cal{E},\cal{F})\times\cal{E}\to\cal{F}\,,\quad\operatorname{ev}_{R}\colon\operatorname{Adj}(\cal{E},\cal{F})^{\operatorname{op}}\times\cal{F}\to\cal{E}

defined by evaluation of the left and right adjoint, respectively.

Proposition 4.11
.

Let
u\colon\cal{I}\to\cal{E}^{\to}
and
v\colon\cal{J}\to\cal{E}^{\to}
be functors.
For a map of adjunctions
\alpha\colon(U,V)\to(U^{\prime},V^{\prime})
, the following are equivalent:

(i)

liftings of
\widehat{\operatorname{ev}}_{L}(\alpha)
making the following diagram commute:

\begin{gathered}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern Corollary 4.12
.

For a functor
u\colon\cal{I}\to\cal{E}^{\to}
and a map of adjunctions
\alpha\colon(U,V)\to(U^{\prime},V^{\prime})
, there is a pullback of categories of the form

\textstyle{{\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\smash{\cal{I}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-10.89029pt\hskip 10.89029pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-10.89029pt\hskip 10.89029pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\smash{\cal{I}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-10.89029pt\hskip 10.89029pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-10.89029pt\hskip 10.89029pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\smash{\cal{I}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-9.051pt\hskip 9.051pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-9.051pt\hskip 9.051pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\smash{\cal{I}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-8.19528pt\hskip 8.19528pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-8.19528pt\hskip 8.19528pt}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\smash{(\widehat{\operatorname{ev}}_{L}(\alpha,-)\mathbin{\circ}u)}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-44.96097pt\hskip 44.96097pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-44.96097pt\hskip 44.96097pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\smash{(\widehat{\operatorname{ev}}_{L}(\alpha,-)\mathbin{\circ}u)}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-44.96097pt\hskip 44.96097pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-44.96097pt\hskip 44.96097pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\smash{(\widehat{\operatorname{ev}}_{L}(\alpha,-)\mathbin{\circ}u)}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-29.9822pt\hskip 29.9822pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-29.9822pt\hskip 29.9822pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\smash{(\widehat{\operatorname{ev}}_{L}(\alpha,-)\mathbin{\circ}u)}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-23.82658pt\hskip 23.82658pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-23.82658pt\hskip 23.82658pt}}}}}
\textstyle{{\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\smash{\cal{I}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-10.89029pt\hskip 10.89029pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-10.89029pt\hskip 10.89029pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\smash{\cal{I}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-10.89029pt\hskip 10.89029pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-10.89029pt\hskip 10.89029pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\smash{\cal{I}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-9.051pt\hskip 9.051pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-9.051pt\hskip 9.051pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\smash{\cal{I}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-8.19528pt\hskip 8.19528pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-8.19528pt\hskip 8.19528pt}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\smash{u}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-8.81627pt\hskip 8.81627pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-8.81627pt\hskip 8.81627pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\smash{u}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-8.81627pt\hskip 8.81627pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-8.81627pt\hskip 8.81627pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\smash{u}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.3435pt\hskip 5.3435pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.3435pt\hskip 5.3435pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\smash{u}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-3.81679pt\hskip 3.81679pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-3.81679pt\hskip 3.81679pt}}}}}
\textstyle{\cal{F}^{\to}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widehat{\operatorname{ev}}_{R}(\alpha,-)}
\textstyle{\cal{E}^{\to}\hbox to0.0pt{.\hss}}

Remark 4.13
.

Recall the adjunction between the cylinder and cocylinder functors in (
1.4
) and write
\bar{\delta}^{k}\colon\exp(I,-)\to\operatorname{Id}
for the mate of
\delta^{k}\colon\operatorname{Id}\to I\otimes(-)
, for
k\in\{0\,,1\}
, so that
\alpha=_{\operatorname{def}}(\delta^{k},\bar{\delta}^{k})
is a map of adjunctions from
\operatorname{Id}\dashv\operatorname{Id}
to
I\otimes(-)\dashv\exp(I,-)
.
Inspecting (
2.2
), we see that the left adjoint
\delta^{k}\mathbin{\hat{\otimes}}(-)
in (
2.5
) is
\widehat{\operatorname{ev}}_{L}(\alpha,-)
.
Similarly, inspecting (
2.4
), we see that the right adjoint
\operatorname{\widehat{exp}}(\bar{\delta}^{k},-)
in (
2.5
) is
\widehat{\operatorname{ev}}_{R}(\alpha,-)
.
This shows that the adjunction (
2.5
) fits into the setting of
propositions

4.11
and
4.12
.

Next, we record some facts about the interaction between orthogonality functors and Kan extensions along fully faithful functors.
These follow immediately from
[
7
, Lemma 24]
.

Proposition 4.14
.

Let
F\colon\cal{I}\to\cal{J}
be a fully faithful functor.

(i)

Assume that the pointwise left Kan extension of
u\colon\cal{I}\to\cal{E}^{\to}
along
F
exists:

\textstyle{\cal{I}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

\scriptstyle{F}
\textstyle{\cal{J}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

\textstyle{\cal{E}^{\to}\hbox to0.0pt{.\hss}}

Then the functor
{\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\smash{F}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-13.0pt\hskip 13.0pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-13.0pt\hskip 13.0pt}}}{{\hskip 10.23616pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\smash{F}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-8.7223pt\hskip 8.7223pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip , fitting in the diagram

\textstyle{{\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\smash{\cal{I}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-10.89029pt\hskip 10.89029pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-10.89029pt\hskip 10.89029pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\smash{\cal{I}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-10.89029pt\hskip 10.89029pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-10.89029pt\hskip 10.89029pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\smash{\cal{I}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-9.051pt\hskip 9.051pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-9.051pt\hskip 9.051pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\smash{\cal{I}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-8.19528pt\hskip 8.19528pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-8.19528pt\hskip 8.19528pt}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\smash{u}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-8.81627pt\hskip 8.81627pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-8.81627pt\hskip 8.81627pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\smash{u}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-8.81627pt\hskip 8.81627pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-8.81627pt\hskip 8.81627pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\smash{u}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.3435pt\hskip 5.3435pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.3435pt\hskip 5.3435pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\smash{u}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-3.81679pt\hskip 3.81679pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-3.81679pt\hskip 3.81679pt}}}}}
