is an isomorphism.

(ii)

Assume that the pointwise right Kan extension of
u\colon\cal{I}\to\cal{E}^{\to}
along
F
exists:

\textstyle{\cal{I}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{u}
\scriptstyle{F}
\textstyle{\cal{J}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{Ran}_{F}u}
\textstyle{\cal{E}^{\to}\hbox to0.0pt{.\hss}}

Then the functor
{\mathchoice{{\hskip 5.3889pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\smash{F}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip-5.3889pt\hskip-13.0pt\pitchfork\hskip 13.0pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-13.0pt\hskip 13.0pt}}}{{\hskip 5.3889pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\smash{F}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip-5.3889pt\hskip-13.0pt\pitchfork\hskip 13.0pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-13.0pt\hskip 13.0pt}}}{{\hskip 4.72229pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\smash{F}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-4.72229pt\hskip-10.23616pt\pitchfork\hskip 10.23616pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-10.23616pt\hskip 10.23616pt}}}{{\hskip 4.72229pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\smash{F}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-4.72229pt\hskip-8.7223pt\pitchfork\hskip 8.7223pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-8.7223pt\hskip 8.7223pt}}}}\colon{\mathchoice{{\hskip 5.3889pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\smash{\cal{J}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip-5.3889pt\hskip-12.22295pt\pitchfork\hskip 12.22295pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-12.22295pt\hskip 12.22295pt}}}{{\hskip 5.3889pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\smash{\cal{J}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip-5.3889pt\hskip-12.22295pt\pitchfork\hskip 12.22295pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-12.22295pt\hskip 12.22295pt}}}{{\hskip 4.72229pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\smash{\cal{J}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-4.72229pt\hskip-10.00053pt\pitchfork\hskip 10.00053pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-10.00053pt\hskip 10.00053pt}}}{{\hskip 4.72229pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\smash{\cal{J}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-4.72229pt\hskip-8.88936pt\pitchfork\hskip 8.88936pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-8.88936pt\hskip 8.88936pt}}}}\to{\mathchoice{{\hskip 5.3889pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\smash{\cal{I}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip-5.3889pt\hskip-10.89029pt\pitchfork\hskip 10.89029pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-10.89029pt\hskip 10.89029pt}}}{{\hskip 5.3889pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\smash{\cal{I}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip-5.3889pt\hskip-10.89029pt\pitchfork\hskip 10.89029pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-10.89029pt\hskip 10.89029pt}}}{{\hskip 4.72229pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\smash{\cal{I}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-4.72229pt\hskip-9.051pt\pitchfork\hskip 9.051pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-9.051pt\hskip 9.051pt}}}{{\hskip 4.72229pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\smash{\cal{I}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-4.72229pt\hskip-8.19528pt\pitchfork\hskip 8.19528pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-8.19528pt\hskip 8.19528pt}}}}
, fitting in the diagram

\textstyle{{\mathchoice{{\hskip 5.3889pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\smash{\cal{I}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip-5.3889pt\hskip-10.89029pt\pitchfork\hskip 10.89029pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-10.89029pt\hskip 10.89029pt}}}{{\hskip 5.3889pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\smash{\cal{I}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip-5.3889pt\hskip-10.89029pt\pitchfork\hskip 10.89029pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-10.89029pt\hskip 10.89029pt}}}{{\hskip 4.72229pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\smash{\cal{I}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-4.72229pt\hskip-9.051pt\pitchfork\hskip 9.051pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-9.051pt\hskip 9.051pt}}}{{\hskip 4.72229pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\smash{\cal{I}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-4.72229pt\hskip-8.19528pt\pitchfork\hskip 8.19528pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-8.19528pt\hskip 8.19528pt}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\mathchoice{{\hskip 3.26666pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\smash{u}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip-3.26666pt\hskip-8.81627pt\pitchfork\hskip 8.81627pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-8.81627pt\hskip 8.81627pt}}}{{\hskip 3.26666pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\smash{u}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip-3.26666pt\hskip-8.81627pt\pitchfork\hskip 8.81627pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-8.81627pt\hskip 8.81627pt}}}{{\hskip 2.33333pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\smash{u}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-2.33333pt\hskip-5.3435pt\pitchfork\hskip 5.3435pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.3435pt\hskip 5.3435pt}}}{{\hskip 2.33333pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\smash{u}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-2.33333pt\hskip-3.81679pt\pitchfork\hskip 3.81679pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-3.81679pt\hskip 3.81679pt}}}}}
\textstyle{{\mathchoice{{\hskip 5.3889pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\smash{\cal{J}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip-5.3889pt\hskip-12.22295pt\pitchfork\hskip 12.22295pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-12.22295pt\hskip 12.22295pt}}}{{\hskip 5.3889pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\smash{\cal{J}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip-5.3889pt\hskip-12.22295pt\pitchfork\hskip 12.22295pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-12.22295pt\hskip 12.22295pt}}}{{\hskip 4.72229pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\smash{\cal{J}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-4.72229pt\hskip-10.00053pt\pitchfork\hskip 10.00053pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-10.00053pt\hskip 10.00053pt}}}{{\hskip 4.72229pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\smash{\cal{J}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-4.72229pt\hskip-8.88936pt\pitchfork\hskip 8.88936pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-8.88936pt\hskip 8.88936pt}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\mathchoice{{\hskip 3.26666pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\smash{F}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip-3.26666pt\hskip-10.23616pt\pitchfork\hskip 10.23616pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-10.23616pt\hskip 10.23616pt}}}{{\hskip 3.26666pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\smash{F}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip-3.26666pt\hskip-10.23616pt\pitchfork\hskip 10.23616pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-10.23616pt\hskip 10.23616pt}}}{{\hskip 2.33333pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\smash{F}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-2.33333pt\hskip-6.37pt\pitchfork\hskip 6.37pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-6.37pt\hskip 6.37pt}}}{{\hskip 2.33333pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\smash{F}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-2.33333pt\hskip-4.54999pt\pitchfork\hskip 4.54999pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-4.54999pt\hskip 4.54999pt}}}}}
\scriptstyle{{\mathchoice{{\hskip 3.26666pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\smash{(\operatorname{Ran}_{F}u)}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip-3.26666pt\hskip-33.60622pt\pitchfork\hskip 33.60622pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-33.60622pt\hskip 33.60622pt}}}{{\hskip 3.26666pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\smash{(\operatorname{Ran}_{F}u)}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip-3.26666pt\hskip-33.60622pt\pitchfork\hskip 33.60622pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-33.60622pt\hskip 33.60622pt}}}{{\hskip 2.33333pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\smash{(\operatorname{Ran}_{F}u)}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-2.33333pt\hskip-21.17058pt\pitchfork\hskip 21.17058pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-21.17058pt\hskip 21.17058pt}}}{{\hskip 2.33333pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\smash{(\operatorname{Ran}_{F}u)}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-2.33333pt\hskip-16.04665pt\pitchfork\hskip 16.04665pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-16.04665pt\hskip 16.04665pt}}}}}
\textstyle{\cal{E}^{\to}\hbox to0.0pt{,\hss}}

is an isomorphism.

We conclude this section by proving two results in the special case that
\cal{E}
is a presheaf category, say
\cal{E}=\mathrm{Psh}(\mathbb{C})
for
\mathbb{C}
a small category.
We write
\mathrm{y}\colon\mathbb{C}\to\cal{E}
for the Yoneda embedding.
Recall from
section

1
that we write
\cal{E}_{\text{{cart}}}^{\to}
for the category of arrows and pullback squares in
\cal{E}
.

Lemma 4.15
.

Let
\cal{J}
be a full subcategory of
\cal{E}_{\text{{cart}}}^{\to}
closed under base change to representables,
i.e.
closed under pullback along morphisms with domain a representable presheaf.
Let
\cal{I}
denote its restriction to arrows into representables,

\textstyle{\cal{I}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\cal{J}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\cal{E}^{\to}\hbox to0.0pt{.\hss}}

Then the inclusion
\cal{J}\to\cal{E}^{\to}
is the left Kan extension of
\cal{I}\to\cal{E}^{\to}
along
\cal{I}\to\cal{J}
.

Since
\cal{E}^{\to}
is cocomplete, we can verify the claim using the colimit formula for left Kan extensions.
All of the following will be functorial in an object
j\colon A\to B
of
\cal{J}
.
We consider the diagram
d\colon S\to\cal{E}^{\to}
, where
S
is the set of pullback squares of the form

\textstyle{A^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i}
\textstyle{A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{j}
\textstyle{\mathrm{y}(x)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{b}
\textstyle{B}

with
i\colon A^{\prime}\to\mathrm{y}(x)
in
\cal{I}
, and the function
d
maps such a square to
i\in\cal{E}^{\to}
.
Our goal is to show that its colimit is
j
.
Using the assumption that
\cal{J}
is closed under base change to representables, the given diagram can be described equivalently as the diagram indexed by maps
b\colon\mathrm{y}(x)\to B
and valued
b^{*}(j)
.
The claim can then be restated as
\operatorname{colim}_{b\colon\mathrm{y}(x)\to B}b^{*}(j)\cong j
, which holds since pullback commutes with colimits in presheaf categories, and
\operatorname{colim}_{b\colon\mathrm{y}(x)\to B}\mathrm{y}(x)\cong B
.
∎

Proposition 4.16
.

Let
\cal{J}
be a full subcategory of
\cal{E}_{\text{{cart}}}^{\to}
closed under base change to representables.
Let
\cal{I}
denote its restriction to arrows into representables,

\textstyle{\cal{I}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\cal{J}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\cal{E}^{\to}\hbox to0.0pt{.\hss}}

Then
{\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\smash{\cal{I}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-10.89029pt\hskip 10.89029pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-10.89029pt\hskip 10.89029pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\smash{\cal{I}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-10.89029pt\hskip 10.89029pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-10.89029pt\hskip 10.89029pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\smash{\cal{I}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-9.051pt\hskip 9.051pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-9.051pt\hskip 9.051pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\smash{\cal{I}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-8.19528pt\hskip 8.19528pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-8.19528pt\hskip 8.19528pt}}}}={\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\smash{\cal{J}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-12.22295pt\hskip 12.22295pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-12.22295pt\hskip 12.22295pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\smash{\cal{J}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-12.22295pt\hskip 12.22295pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-12.22295pt\hskip 12.22295pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\smash{\cal{J}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-10.00053pt\hskip 10.00053pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-10.00053pt\hskip 10.00053pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\smash{\cal{J}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-8.88936pt\hskip 8.88936pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-8.88936pt\hskip 8.88936pt}}}}
.

The result follows by combining part (i) of
proposition

4.14
and
lemma

4.15
.
∎

5.
The functorial Frobenius condition

Recall that a
functorial factorization
on a category
\cal{E}
consist of a pair of functors
\mathsf{L},\mathsf{R}\colon\cal{E}^{\to}\to\cal{E}^{\to}
providing factorizations

\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{L}f}
\textstyle{Y}
\textstyle{\mathsf{M}f\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{R}f}

functorially in
f\in\cal{E}^{\to}
.
The functors
\mathsf{L}
and
\mathsf{R}
then admit a canonical copointing and pointing, respectively, given by the natural transformations
(\operatorname{Id},\mathsf{R})\colon\mathsf{L}\to\operatorname{Id}
and
(\mathsf{L},\operatorname{Id})\colon\operatorname{Id}\to\mathsf{R}
.
We write
{\mathsf{L}}\text{-}\mathsf{map}
for the category of copointed endofunctor algebras over
\mathsf{L}
and
{\mathsf{R}}\text{-}\mathsf{map}
for the category of pointed endofunctor algebras over
\mathsf{R}
.
Both of these categories have evident forgetful functors into
\cal{E}^{\to}
.

For an
algebraic weak factorization system
(awfs)
[
16
,
20
]
, we require also a comultiplication
\mathsf{L}\to\mathsf{L}\mathsf{L}
and a multiplication
\mathsf{R}\mathsf{R}\to\mathsf{R}
, giving the structure of a comonad on
\mathsf{L}
and of a monad on
\mathsf{R}
, respectively, such that the canonical natural transformation
\mathsf{L}\mathsf{R}\to\mathsf{R}\mathsf{L}
is a distributive law.
We write
{\mathsf{L}}\text{-}\mathsf{Map}
for the category of comonad coalgebras over
\mathsf{L}
and
{\mathsf{R}}\text{-}\mathsf{Map}
for the category of monad algebras over
\mathsf{R}
.
These come equipped with forgetful functors into
\cal{E}^{\to}
and fulfill

{\mathsf{L}}\text{-}\mathsf{map}={\mathchoice{{\hskip 5.3889pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\smash{{\mathsf{R}}\text{-}\mathsf{Map}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip-5.3889pt\hskip-33.69452pt\pitchfork\hskip 33.69452pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-33.69452pt\hskip 33.69452pt}}}{{\hskip 5.3889pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\smash{{\mathsf{R}}\text{-}\mathsf{Map}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip-5.3889pt\hskip-33.69452pt\pitchfork\hskip 33.69452pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-33.69452pt\hskip 33.69452pt}}}{{\hskip 4.72229pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\smash{{\mathsf{R}}\text{-}\mathsf{Map}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-4.72229pt\hskip-24.4091pt\pitchfork\hskip 24.4091pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-24.4091pt\hskip 24.4091pt}}}{{\hskip 4.72229pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\smash{{\mathsf{R}}\text{-}\mathsf{Map}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-4.72229pt\hskip-18.46883pt\pitchfork\hskip 18.46883pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-18.46883pt\hskip 18.46883pt}}}}\,,\quad{\mathsf{R}}\text{-}\mathsf{map}={\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\smash{{\mathsf{L}}\text{-}\mathsf{Map}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-32.65285pt\hskip 32.65285pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-32.65285pt\hskip 32.65285pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\smash{{\mathsf{L}}\text{-}\mathsf{Map}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-32.65285pt\hskip 32.65285pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-32.65285pt\hskip 32.65285pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\smash{{\mathsf{L}}\text{-}\mathsf{Map}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-23.67993pt\hskip 23.67993pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-23.67993pt\hskip 23.67993pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\smash{{\mathsf{L}}\text{-}\mathsf{Map}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-17.948pt\hskip 17.948pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-17.948pt\hskip 17.948pt}}}}\,.

An awfs
(\mathsf{L},\mathsf{R})
has an underlying ordinary wfs, in which the left (right) class consists of the maps that admit the structure making them into an element of
{\mathsf{L}}\text{-}\mathsf{map}
(of
{\mathsf{R}}\text{-}\mathsf{map}
, respectively).
Recall also that an awfs
(\mathsf{L},\mathsf{R})
is said to be
algebraically-free
on
u\colon\cal{I}\to\cal{E}^{\to}
if
{\mathsf{R}}\text{-}\mathsf{Map}\cong{\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\smash{\cal{I}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-10.89029pt\hskip 10.89029pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-10.89029pt\hskip 10.89029pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\smash{\cal{I}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-10.89029pt\hskip 10.89029pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-10.89029pt\hskip 10.89029pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\smash{\cal{I}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-9.051pt\hskip 9.051pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-9.051pt\hskip 9.051pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\smash{\cal{I}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-8.19528pt\hskip 8.19528pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-8.19528pt\hskip 8.19528pt}}}}
.
By Garner’s small object argument
[
16
]
, every
u\colon\cal{I}\to\cal{E}^{\to}
with
\cal{I}
small and
\cal{E}
locally-presentable determines an awfs
(\mathsf{L},\mathsf{R})
that is algebraically-free on
\cal{I}
.
We call such an awfs
cofibrantly generated
.

We now state the analogue for an awfs of the Frobenius condition for a wfs.
This condition, called the functorial Frobenius condition, was stated for cloven wfs’s in
[
3
]
, which are slightly more general than awfs’s.
Let us fix some notation.
For a category
\cal{E}
, we write
\cal{E}^{\to}\times{E}\cal{E}^{\to}
for the pullback of
\operatorname{cod}\colon\cal{E}^{\to}\to\cal{E}
with itself.
This is the category of cospans in
\cal{E}
,
i.e.
pairs of arrows with common codomain.
Pullback gives a functor
P\colon\cal{E}^{\to}\times_{\cal{E}}\cal{E}^{\to}\to\cal{E}^{\to}
sending a cospan
(g,h)
to
h^{*}(g)
.

Definition 5.1
.

An awfs
(\mathsf{L},\mathsf{R})
in
\cal{E}
satisfies the
functorial Frobenius condition
if pullback along
{\mathsf{R}}\text{-}\mathsf{map}
preserves
{\mathsf{L}}\text{-}\mathsf{map}
, in the sense that we have a lift
\tilde{P}
of
P
, as follows:

\textstyle{{\mathsf{L}}\text{-}\mathsf{map}\times_{\cal{E}}{\mathsf{R}}\text{-}\mathsf{map}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\tilde{P}}
\textstyle{{\mathsf{L}}\text{-}\mathsf{map}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\cal{E}^{\to}\times_{\cal{E}}\cal{E}^{\to}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{P}
\textstyle{\cal{E}^{\to}\hbox to0.0pt{.\hss}}

In this section, we obtain an analogue of
proposition

1.4
for awfs’s,
i.e.
a necessary and sufficient condition for an algebraically-free awfs to satisfy the functorial Frobenius condition (
theorem

5.9
).
We will apply this characterization in the next section in order to prove a functorial Frobenius property for the awfs having uniform fibrations as right maps.

In order to prove the desired characterization, it is convenient to work with a variant of the functorial Frobenius condition we introduce in
definition

5.2
below.
For this, we introduce some notation.
Given
u\colon\cal{I}\to\cal{E}^{\to}
, we write
\cal{I}_{/\cal{E}}
for the category with objects consisting of tuples
(X,i,a,b)
with
X\in\cal{E}
,
i\in\cal{I}
(giving a map
u_{i}\colon A_{i}\to B_{i}
),
a\colon A_{i}\to X
,
b\colon B_{i}\to X
such that the following diagram commutes:

\textstyle{A_{i}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{a}
\scriptstyle{u_{i}}
\textstyle{B_{i}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{b}
\textstyle{X\hbox to0.0pt{.\hss}}

We write
s\colon\cal{I}_{/\cal{E}}\to\cal{E}
for the first projection.
Then, for
v\colon\cal{J}\to\cal{E}^{\to}
, we can form a pullback

\textstyle{\cal{I}_{/\cal{E}}\times_{\cal{E}}\cal{J}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\cal{J}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{cod}\mathbin{\circ}v}
\textstyle{\cal{I}_{/\cal{E}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{s}
\textstyle{\cal{E}\hbox to0.0pt{.\hss}}

When
u
and
v
are the identity on
\cal{E}^{\to}
, we obtain

\textstyle{\cal{E}_{/\cal{E}}^{\to}\times_{\cal{E}}\cal{E}^{\to}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\cal{E}^{\to}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{cod}}
\textstyle{\cal{E}^{\to}_{/\cal{E}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{s}
\textstyle{\cal{E}\hbox to0.0pt{.\hss}}

Pullback gives a functor
Q\colon\cal{E}^{\to}_{/\cal{E}}\times_{\cal{E}}\cal{E}^{\to}\to\cal{E}_{/\cal{E}}^{\to}
sending

(f,h)
to
h^{*}(f)
.

Definition 5.2
.

Let
u\colon\cal{I}\to\cal{E}^{\to}
,
v\colon\cal{J}\to\cal{E}^{\to}
,
w\colon\cal{K}\to\cal{E}^{\to}
be categories of arrows.
We say that
(\cal{I},\cal{J},\cal{K})
satisfies the
generalized functorial Frobenius condition
if pullback along
\cal{J}
-maps sends
\cal{I}
-maps to
\cal{K}
-maps,
i.e.
the functor
Q
admits a lift
\tilde{Q}
as below:

(5.1)

\begin{gathered}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 33.34053pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-19.80598pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern to0.0pt{.\hss}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{gathered}

The next proposition shows in what sense the generalized functorial Frobenius condition is functorial in its parameters.

Proposition 5.3
.

Consider functors
u_{t}\colon\cal{I}_{t}\to\cal{E}^{\to}
,
v_{t}\colon\cal{J}_{t}\to\cal{E}^{\to}
,
w_{t}\colon\cal{K}_{t}\to\cal{E}^{\to}
, for
t\in\{1\,,2\}
, related as follows:

\textstyle{\cal{I}_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{F}
\scriptstyle{u_{2}}
\textstyle{\cal{I}_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{u_{1}}
\textstyle{\cal{E}^{\to}\hbox to0.0pt{,\hss}}

\textstyle{\cal{J}_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{G}
\scriptstyle{v_{2}}
\textstyle{\cal{J}_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{v_{1}}
\textstyle{\cal{E}^{\to}\hbox to0.0pt{,\hss}}

\textstyle{\cal{K}_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{H}
\scriptstyle{w_{1}}
\textstyle{\cal{K}_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{w_{2}}
\textstyle{\cal{E}^{\to}\hbox to0.0pt{.\hss}}

If
(\cal{I}_{1},\cal{J}_{1},\cal{K}_{1})
satisfies the generalized functorial Frobenius condition, then so does
(\cal{I}_{2},\cal{J}_{2},\cal{K}_{2})
.
∎

Next, we show how the generalized functorial Frobenius condition can be simplified under some mild assumptions.

Proposition 5.4
.

Let
u\colon\cal{I}\to\cal{E}^{\to}
,
v\colon\cal{J}\to\cal{E}^{\to}
,
w\colon\cal{K}\to\cal{E}^{\to}
be categories of arrows such that

(5.2)

is a morphism of Grothendieck fibrations.
Then the following are equivalent:

(i)

the triple

satisfies the generalized functorial Frobenius condition,

(ii)

there is a lift
\tilde{P}
as below:

(5.3)

\begin{gathered}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 63.61453pt\raise-32.19112pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\cal{E}^{\to}\hbox to0.0pt{.\hss}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{gathered}

We will show that giving lifts
\tilde{Q}
and
\tilde{P}
as in (
5.1
) and (
5.3
), respectively, is equivalent.
The situation (
5.1
) a priori represents a more general scenario: we are pulling back an arrow over an object
Y
,

\textstyle{A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{u_{i}}
\textstyle{B\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Y\hbox to0.0pt{,\hss}}

with
i\in\cal{I}
, along a map
v_{j}\colon X\to Y
with
j\in\cal{J}
.
In contrast, in (
5.3
) we restrict to the case that the map
B\to Y
is the identity.
It follows that any lift
\tilde{Q}
induces a lift
\tilde{P}
.
However, under the stated assumption that (
5.2
) is a morphism of Grothendieck fibrations, the lift
\tilde{Q}
can be reconstructed from the lift
\tilde{P}
by first pulling back
v_{j}
along
B\to Y
to a map
v_{j^{\prime}}\colon D\to B
with
j^{\prime}\to j
a map in
\cal{J}
in the above situation and then setting
\tilde{Q}(i,j)=_{\operatorname{def}}\tilde{P}(i,j^{\prime})
.
∎

We can now restate the functorial Frobenius condition of
definition

5.1
equivalently in terms of the generalized functorial Frobenius condition of
definition

5.2
.

∎

The benefit of the reformulation in
proposition

5.5
of the functorial Frobenius condition as an instance of the generalized functorial Frobenius condition is that the latter admits an equivalent rephrasing in terms of pushforward, rather than pullback, functors.
This rephrasing, which does not seem directly possible for the functorial Frobenius condition, will be essential to our characterization of algebraically-free awfs’s satisfying the functorial Frobenius condition.
As a first step towards the pushforward formulation, we decompose the generalized functorial Frobenius condition into an object part and a morphism part.
For this, recall that any arrow
f\colon X\to Y
induces an adjunction
f_{!}\dashv f^{*}
between the slices
\cal{E}_{/X}
and
\cal{E}_{/Y}
given by left composition
f_{!}
and pullback
f^{*}
.
Given a functor
u\colon\cal{I}\to\cal{E}^{\to}
, it is immediate to check that the left composition functor lifts as follows:

\textstyle{\cal{I}_{/X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{f_{!}}}
\scriptstyle{u_{/X}}
\textstyle{\cal{I}_{/Y}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{u_{/Y}}
\textstyle{\cal{E}_{/X}^{\to}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{!}}
\textstyle{\cal{E}_{/Y}^{\to}\hbox to0.0pt{.\hss}}

Note that we can view the bottom map as a lift of
f_{!}\colon\cal{E}_{/X}\to\cal{E}_{/Y}
to arrow categories.

4.72229pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\smash{({\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\smash{\cal{K}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-9.37274pt\hskip is faithful, the lift
\widetilde{Q}
consists just of a lift of the action of
Q
on objects that is coherent (in an evident sense) with respect to the action of
Q
on morphisms, in the sense that the action of
Q
on morphisms then determines the action of
\widetilde{Q}
on morphisms.
Coherence in morphisms in
\cal{I}_{/\cal{E}}\times_{\cal{E}}\cal{J}
of the action on objects of
\widetilde{Q}
separates into two parts:

(1)

coherence in morphisms in
\cal{I}_{/D_{j}}
for fixed
v_{j}\colon C_{j}\to D_{j}
with
j\in\cal{J}
,

(2)

coherence in morphisms in
\cal{J}
.

The action of
\widetilde{Q}
on objects together with coherence (1) constitutes part (i).
Coherence (2) constitutes part (ii).
∎

\textstyle{{{\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\smash{\cal{I}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-10.89029pt\hskip 10.89029pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-10.89029pt\hskip 10.89029pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\smash{\cal{I}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-10.89029pt\hskip 10.89029pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-10.89029pt\hskip 10.89029pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\smash{\cal{I}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-9.051pt\hskip 9.051pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-9.051pt\hskip 9.051pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\smash{\cal{I}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-8.19528pt\hskip 8.19528pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-8.19528pt\hskip 8.19528pt}}}}}_{/D_{k}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{t^{*}}}
\textstyle{{{\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\smash{\cal{K}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-13.06464pt\hskip 13.06464pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-13.06464pt\hskip 13.06464pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\smash{\cal{K}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-13.06464pt\hskip 13.06464pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-13.06464pt\hskip 13.06464pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\smash{\cal{K}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-10.62724pt\hskip 10.62724pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-10.62724pt\hskip 10.62724pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\smash{\cal{K}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-9.37274pt\hskip 9.37274pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-9.37274pt\hskip 9.37274pt}}}}}_{/C_{j}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{(v_{j})_{*}}}
\textstyle{{{\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\smash{\cal{I}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-10.89029pt\hskip 10.89029pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-10.89029pt\hskip 10.89029pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\smash{\cal{I}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-10.89029pt\hskip 10.89029pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-10.89029pt\hskip 10.89029pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\smash{\cal{I}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-9.051pt\hskip 9.051pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-9.051pt\hskip 9.051pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\smash{\cal{I}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-8.19528pt\hskip 8.19528pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-8.19528pt\hskip 8.19528pt}}}}}_{/D_{j}}\hbox to0.0pt{.\hss}}

For (i), note that we can view the bottom map in part (i) of
proposition

5.6
as a lift of
v_{j}^{*}\colon\cal{E}_{/D_{j}}\to\cal{E}_{/C_{j}}
to arrow categories, so we can apply
proposition

4.7
to the adjunction
v_{j}^{*}\dashv(v_{j})_{*}
with
u=u_{/D_{j}}
and
v={{\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\smash{w}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-12.6088pt\hskip 12.6088pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-12.6088pt\hskip 12.6088pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\smash{w}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-12.6088pt\hskip 12.6088pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-12.6088pt\hskip 12.6088pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\smash{w}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-10.06161pt\hskip 10.06161pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-10.06161pt\hskip 10.06161pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\smash{w}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-8.69685pt\hskip 8.69685pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-8.69685pt\hskip 8.69685pt}}}}}_{/C_{j}}
, using
proposition

4.4
to permute slicing and orthogonality functors.
For (ii), apply
proposition

4.8
to the adjunctions
s_{!}v_{j}^{*}\dashv(v_{j})_{*}s^{*}
and
v_{k}^{*}t_{!}\dashv t^{*}(v_{k})_{*}
recalling that
\phi
and
\psi
are mates.
∎

of the adjunction (
4.1
) to induce a lift of pushforward for (ii).
∎

We can finally give the desired characterization of the functorial Frobenius condition for an algebraically-free awfs.

Theorem 5.9
.

Let
(\mathsf{L},\mathsf{R})
be an awfs algebraically-free on
u\colon\cal{I}\to\cal{E}^{\to}
.
Then the following are equivalent:

(i)

(\mathsf{L},\mathsf{R})
satisfies the functorial Frobenius condition,

(ii)

(\cal{I},{\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\smash{\cal{I}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-10.89029pt\hskip 10.89029pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 8.19528pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-8.19528pt\hskip 8.19528pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\smash{\cal{I}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-3.81158pt\hskip 3.81158pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-3.81158pt\hskip 3.81158pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\smash{\cal{I}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-2.72256pt\hskip 2.72256pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-2.72256pt\hskip 2.72256pt}}}})}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-4.72229pt\hskip-54.08119pt\pitchfork\hskip 54.08119pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-54.08119pt\hskip 54.08119pt}}}})=(\cal{I},{\mathsf{R}}\text{-}\mathsf{Map},{\mathsf{L}}\text{-}\mathsf{map})
satisfies the generalized functorial Frobenius condition.

Using
proposition

4.2
and the adjunction (
4.1
), we have
\overline{{\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\smash{\cal{I}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-10.89029pt\hskip 10.89029pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-10.89029pt\hskip 10.89029pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\smash{\cal{I}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-10.89029pt\hskip 10.89029pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-10.89029pt\hskip 10.89029pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\smash{\cal{I}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-9.051pt\hskip 9.051pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-9.051pt\hskip 9.051pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\smash{\cal{I}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-8.19528pt\hskip 8.19528pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-8.19528pt\hskip 8.19528pt}}}}}\leftrightarrow{\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\smash{\cal{I}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-10.89029pt\hskip 10.89029pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-10.89029pt\hskip 10.89029pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\smash{\cal{I}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-10.89029pt\hskip 10.89029pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-10.89029pt\hskip 10.89029pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\smash{\cal{I}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-9.051pt\hskip 9.051pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-9.051pt\hskip 9.051pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\smash{\cal{I}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-8.19528pt\hskip 8.19528pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-8.19528pt\hskip 8.19528pt}}}}
, hence
{\mathsf{R}}\text{-}\mathsf{Map}\leftrightarrow\overline{{\mathsf{R}}\text{-}\mathsf{Map}}
.
Note that
\overline{{\mathsf{R}}\text{-}\mathsf{Map}}\leftrightarrow{\mathsf{R}}\text{-}\mathsf{map}
(this is the case for pointed endofunctor and monad algebras over any monad).
It follows that
{\mathsf{R}}\text{-}\mathsf{Map}\leftrightarrow{\mathsf{R}}\text{-}\mathsf{map}
.

With this, the functorial Frobenius condition for
(\mathsf{L},\mathsf{R})
can be stated equivalently as the existence of a lift of the form

by
proposition

, which is equivalent to the generalized functorial Frobenius condition for
(\cal{I},{\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\smash{\cal{I}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-10.89029pt\hskip 10.89029pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 8.19528pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-8.19528pt\hskip 8.19528pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\smash{\cal{I}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-8.19528pt\hskip 8.19528pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-8.19528pt\hskip 8.19528pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\smash{\cal{I}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-3.81158pt\hskip 3.81158pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-3.81158pt\hskip 3.81158pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\smash{\cal{I}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-2.72256pt\hskip 2.72256pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-2.72256pt\hskip 2.72256pt}}}})}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-4.72229pt\hskip-54.08119pt\pitchfork\hskip 54.08119pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-54.08119pt\hskip 54.08119pt}}}})
by
proposition

5.8
.
∎

We conclude the section with a result needed in
section

7
.

Proposition 5.10
.

Let
u_{t}\colon\cal{I}_{t}\to\cal{E}^{\to}
,
w_{t}\colon\cal{K}_{t}\to\cal{E}^{\to}
for
t\in\{1\,,2\}
and
v\colon\cal{J}\to\cal{E}^{\to}
.
If
(\cal{I}_{1},\cal{J},\cal{K}_{1},)
and
(\cal{I}_{2},\cal{J},\cal{K}_{2})
satisfy the generalized functorial Frobenius property, then so do

(\cal{I}_{1}\times_{\cal{E}^{\to}}\cal{I}_{2},\cal{J},\cal{K}_{1}\times_{\cal{E}^{\to}}\cal{K}_{2})\,,\quad(\cal{I}_{1}+_{\cal{E}^{\to}}\cal{I}_{2},\cal{J},\cal{K}_{1}+_{\cal{E}^{\to}}\cal{K}_{2})\,.

This follows easily from
proposition

5.3
using the universal property of products and coproducts in the category
\mathbf{CAT}_{/\cal{E}^{\to}}
.
∎

This concludes our work on orthogonality functors and the functorial Frobenius condition in general.
In
sections

6
and
7
, we develop the algebraic counterpart of the material in
sections

2
and
3
.

6.
Uniform fibrations

This section is devoted to introducing the notion of a uniform fibration, which is the algebraic counterpart of the notion of a fibration introduced in
definition

2.3
, and establishing some basic results about it.
For this, we first introduce suitable awfs’s, in complete analogy with the way we defined suitable wfs’s in
definition

2.1
.
We will use the notation
(\mathsf{C},\mathsf{F_{t}})
to denote a suitable awfs and let

(6.1)

\mathsf{Cof}=_{\operatorname{def}}{\mathsf{C}}\text{-}\mathsf{map}\,,\quad\mathsf{TrivFib}=_{\operatorname{def}}{\mathsf{F_{t}}}\text{-}\mathsf{Map}\,.

We then refer to objects in
\mathsf{Cof}
as
uniform cofibrations
and objects in
\mathsf{TrivFib}
as
uniform trivial fibrations
.
Again, we hope that this helps to convey some of the basic intuition motivating our development.
In (
6.1
), the dichotomy between copointed endofunctor coalgebras on one hand and monad algebras on the other is motivated by our technical development: the uniform fibrations introduced in
definition

6.3
, which generalize the uniform fibrations in cubical sets in
[
11
]
, are monad algebras (as shown in
theorem

6.5
) rather than pointed endofunctor algebras.
Note however that we will only consider cofibrantly generated awfs
(\mathsf{L},\mathsf{R})
, for which there are always functors back and forth between
{\mathsf{R}}\text{-}\mathsf{Map}
and
{\mathsf{R}}\text{-}\mathsf{map}
.

Definition 6.1
.

An awfs
(\mathsf{C},\mathsf{F_{t}})
is said to be
suitable
if the following conditions hold.

(S1)

(\mathsf{C},\mathsf{F_{t}})
is cofibrantly generated.

(S2)

The functor
\bot\colon\cal{E}\to\cal{E}^{\to}
mapping
X\in\cal{E}
to
\bot_{X}\colon 0_{\cal{E}}\to X
factors through
\mathsf{Cof}
, as in the diagram

\textstyle{\cal{E}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\bot}}
\scriptstyle{\bot}
\textstyle{\mathsf{Cof}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\cal{E}^{\to}\hbox to0.0pt{.\hss}}

(S3)

\mathsf{Cof}
is closed under pullback, in the sense that the pullback functor
P
of
section

5
lifts as follows:

\textstyle{\mathsf{Cof}\times_{\cal{E}}\cal{E}^{\to}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{P}}
\textstyle{\mathsf{Cof}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\cal{E}^{\to}\times_{\cal{E}}\cal{E}^{\to}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{P}
\textstyle{\cal{E}^{\to}\hbox to0.0pt{.\hss}}

(S4)

\mathsf{Cof}
is closed under Leibniz product with endpoint inclusions, in the sense that the Leibniz product functor lifts as follows for
k\in\{0\,,1\}
:

\textstyle{\mathsf{Cof}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\delta^{k}\mathbin{\hat{\otimes}}(-)}}
\textstyle{\mathsf{Cof}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\cal{E}^{\to}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\delta^{k}\mathbin{\hat{\otimes}}(-)}
\textstyle{\cal{E}^{\to}\hbox to0.0pt{.\hss}}

The main difference between the notion of a suitable wfs (
definition

2.1
) and that of a suitable awfs (
definition

6.1
) is that the conditions in the former express closure of the class of left maps under the action of certain functions, while the conditions in the latter express closure of the category of left maps under the action of certain functors.

Note that, by
proposition

5.4
, condition (S3) is equivalent to the generalized functorial Frobenius condition for
(\mathsf{Cof},\cal{E}^{\to},\mathsf{Cof})
.

Example 6.2
.

As we will see in
section

8
, the categories
\mathbf{SSet}
and
\mathbf{CSet}
admit suitable awfs’s
(\mathsf{C},\mathsf{F_{t}})
that are algebraically-free on the categories
\cal{M}
of all monomorphisms and pullback squares,
i.e.
such that
\mathsf{TrivFib}={\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\smash{\cal{M}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-17.45413pt\hskip 17.45413pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-17.45413pt\hskip 17.45413pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\smash{\cal{M}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-17.45413pt\hskip 17.45413pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-17.45413pt\hskip 17.45413pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\smash{\cal{M}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-13.92432pt\hskip 13.92432pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-13.92432pt\hskip 13.92432pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\smash{\cal{M}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-11.93694pt\hskip 11.93694pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-11.93694pt\hskip 11.93694pt}}}}
.
The right maps of these awfs’s are a natural algebraic counterpart of trivial Kan fibrations and so we call them
uniform trivial Kan fibrations
.
Similarly, we will see in
example

8.3
that the category
\mathbf{CSet}
also admits a suitable awfs
(\mathsf{C}^{\prime},\mathsf{F_{t}}^{\prime})
algebraically-free on the category
\cal{M}^{\prime}
of monomorphisms classified by the face lattice
\Phi
of
[
11
]
and pullback squares (see
example

2.7
).

Let us now fix a suitable awfs
(\mathsf{C},\mathsf{F_{t}})
cofibrantly generated by a small category of maps
u\colon\cal{I}\to\cal{E}^{\to}
.
We refer to the objects of
\cal{I}
as
generating uniform cofibrations
.
For
k\in\{0\,,1\}
, we define a functor
\delta^{k}\mathbin{\hat{\otimes}}u\colon\cal{I}\to\cal{E}^{\to}
by letting

(\delta^{k}\mathbin{\hat{\otimes}}u)_{i}=_{\operatorname{def}}\delta^{k}\mathbin{\hat{\otimes}}u_{i}\,,

where
\delta^{k}\mathbin{\hat{\otimes}}u_{i}
is the Leibniz product of
\delta^{k}
and
u_{i}\colon A_{i}\to B_{i}
, as in (
2.1
).
Now let
\cal{I}_{\otimes}=_{\operatorname{def}}\cal{I}+\cal{I}
and define
u_{\otimes}\colon\cal{I}_{\otimes}\to\cal{E}^{\to}
via the coproduct diagram

(6.2)

\begin{gathered}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 81.7991pt\raise 5.00694pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.00694pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\iota_{1}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\cal{E}^{\to}\hbox to0.0pt{.\hss}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{gathered}

We refer to the objects of
\cal{I}_{\otimes}
as
generating uniform trivial cofibrations
.

Definition 6.3
.

(i)

A
uniform fibration
is a right
\cal{I}_{\otimes}
-map.

(ii)

A
morphism of uniform fibrations
is a morphism of right
\cal{I}_{\otimes}
-maps.

We write
\mathsf{Fib}
for the category of uniform fibrations and their morphisms.
We now give the algebraic analogue of
proposition

2.4
,
i.e.
an equivalent characterization of uniform fibrations which does not refer to generating uniform cofibrations.
This will show that the category
\mathsf{Fib}
is independent from the choice of the functor
u\colon\cal{I}\to\cal{E}^{\to}
cofibrantly generating
(\mathsf{C},\mathsf{F_{t}})
, and instead depends only on the awfs
(\mathsf{C},\mathsf{F_{t}})
.

Proposition 6.4
.

Uniform fibration structures on
p\in\cal{E}^{\to}
are isomorphic to uniform trivial fibration structures on both
\operatorname{\widehat{exp}}(\bar{\delta}^{0},p)
and
\operatorname{\widehat{exp}}(\bar{\delta}^{1},p)
, naturally in
p
.
More precisely, the category
\mathsf{Fib}
is isomorphic over
\cal{E}^{\to}
to the product of the categories
\cal{D}_{0}
and
\cal{D}_{1}
defined via pullbacks as follows:

\textstyle{\cal{D}_{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathsf{TrivFib}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\cal{E}^{\to}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{\widehat{exp}}(\bar{\delta}^{0},p)}
\textstyle{\cal{E}^{\to}\hbox to0.0pt{,\hss}}

\textstyle{\cal{D}_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathsf{TrivFib}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\cal{E}^{\to}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{\widehat{exp}}(\bar{\delta}^{1},p)}
\textstyle{\cal{E}^{\to}\hbox to0.0pt{.\hss}}

Recall that
\mathsf{TrivFib}={\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\smash{\cal{I}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-10.89029pt\hskip 10.89029pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-10.89029pt\hskip 10.89029pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\smash{\cal{I}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-10.89029pt\hskip 10.89029pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-10.89029pt\hskip 10.89029pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\smash{\cal{I}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-9.051pt\hskip 9.051pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-9.051pt\hskip 9.051pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\smash{\cal{I}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-8.19528pt\hskip 8.19528pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-8.19528pt\hskip 8.19528pt}}}}
and
\mathsf{Fib}={\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\smash{(\cal{I}_{\otimes})}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-25.4181pt\hskip 25.4181pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-25.4181pt\hskip 25.4181pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\smash{(\cal{I}_{\otimes})}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-25.4181pt\hskip 25.4181pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-25.4181pt\hskip 25.4181pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\smash{(\cal{I}_{\otimes})}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-21.21776pt\hskip 21.21776pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-21.21776pt\hskip 21.21776pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\smash{(\cal{I}_{\otimes})}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-19.52873pt\hskip 19.52873pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-19.52873pt\hskip 19.52873pt}}}}
.
Note that the right orthogonality functor is contravariant and part of the adjunction (
4.1
), hence sends coproducts to products of categories over
\cal{E}^{\to}
.
The statement then follows from
corollary

4.12
via
remark

4.13
.
∎

We write
\mathsf{TrivCof}
for the category of uniform trivial cofibrations, defined as left
\mathsf{Fib}
-maps,
i.e.

\mathsf{Fib}=_{\operatorname{def}}{\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\smash{(\cal{I}_{\otimes})}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-25.4181pt\hskip 25.4181pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-25.4181pt\hskip 25.4181pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\smash{(\cal{I}_{\otimes})}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-25.4181pt\hskip 25.4181pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-25.4181pt\hskip 25.4181pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\smash{(\cal{I}_{\otimes})}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-21.21776pt\hskip 21.21776pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-21.21776pt\hskip 21.21776pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\smash{(\cal{I}_{\otimes})}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-19.52873pt\hskip 19.52873pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-19.52873pt\hskip 19.52873pt}}}}\,,\quad\mathsf{TrivCof}=_{\operatorname{def}}{\mathchoice{{\hskip 5.3889pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\smash{\mathsf{Fib}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip-5.3889pt\hskip-18.43057pt\pitchfork\hskip 18.43057pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-18.43057pt\hskip 18.43057pt}}}{{\hskip 5.3889pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\smash{\mathsf{Fib}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip-5.3889pt\hskip-18.43057pt\pitchfork\hskip 18.43057pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-18.43057pt\hskip 18.43057pt}}}{{\hskip 4.72229pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\smash{\mathsf{Fib}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-4.72229pt\hskip-13.36324pt\pitchfork\hskip 13.36324pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-13.36324pt\hskip 13.36324pt}}}{{\hskip 4.72229pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\smash{\mathsf{Fib}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-4.72229pt\hskip-10.14238pt\pitchfork\hskip 10.14238pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-10.14238pt\hskip 10.14238pt}}}}\,.

The next statement is immediate.

Theorem 6.5
.

The category
\cal{E}
admits a cofibrantly generated awfs
(\mathsf{C_{t}},\mathsf{F})
such that

\mathsf{TrivCof}={\mathsf{C_{t}}}\text{-}\mathsf{map}\,,\quad\mathsf{Fib}={\mathsf{F}}\text{-}\mathsf{Map}\,.

By Garner’s small object argument
[
16
]
(see also
[
7
, Proposition 16]
), applied to the generating small category of arrows
\cal{I}_{\otimes}
.
∎

Note again that there are functors over
\cal{E}^{\to}
back and forth between
{\mathsf{F}}\text{-}\mathsf{map}
and
{\mathsf{F}}\text{-}\mathsf{Map}
since
(\mathsf{C_{t}},\mathsf{F})
is cofibrantly generated.
Let us remark that
proposition

6.4
would not hold as stated above if we had defined uniform (trivial) fibrations as algebras for the pointed endofunctors of the relevant awfs’s; instead of an isomorphism we would have only functors back and forth over
\cal{E}^{\to}
.

Example 6.6
.

If we start from the awfs’s
(\mathsf{C},\mathsf{F_{t}})
in
\mathbf{SSet}
and
\mathbf{CSet}
of
example

6.2
and apply
theorem

6.5
, we obtain awfs’s
(\mathsf{C_{t}},\mathsf{F})
whose monad algebras are algebraic counterparts of Kan fibrations, and hence will be called
uniform Kan fibrations
.
In the case of
\mathbf{CSet}
, if we instead start with the awfs
(\mathsf{C}^{\prime},\mathsf{F_{t}}^{\prime})
of
example

6.2
, we obtain an awfs
(\mathsf{C_{t}}^{\prime},\mathsf{F}^{\prime})
whose right maps are exactly the uniform Kan fibrations considered in
[
11
]
.
An application to a different version of cubical sets (not using connections) gives the awfs considered in
[
38
]
.

The next corollary, which will be useful to establish the functorial Frobenius property for
(\mathsf{C_{t}},\mathsf{F})
in
section

7
, is the algebraic counterpart of
corollary

2.9
.

Corollary 6.7
.

Leibniz product with endpoint inclusions sends uniform cofibrations to uniform trivial cofibrations, in the sense that the Leibniz product functor lifts as follows for
k\in\{0\,,1\}
:

\textstyle{\mathsf{Cof}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathsf{TrivCof}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\cal{E}^{\to}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\delta^{k}\mathbin{\hat{\otimes}}(-)}
\textstyle{\cal{E}^{\to}\hbox to0.0pt{.\hss}}

Recall that
\mathsf{Cof}={\mathchoice{{\hskip 5.3889pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\smash{\mathsf{TrivFib}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip-5.3889pt\hskip-34.8195pt\pitchfork\hskip 34.8195pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-34.8195pt\hskip 34.8195pt}}}{{\hskip 5.3889pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\smash{\mathsf{TrivFib}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip-5.3889pt\hskip-34.8195pt\pitchfork\hskip 34.8195pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-34.8195pt\hskip 34.8195pt}}}{{\hskip 4.72229pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\smash{\mathsf{TrivFib}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-4.72229pt\hskip-24.83548pt\pitchfork\hskip 24.83548pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-24.83548pt\hskip 24.83548pt}}}{{\hskip 4.72229pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\smash{\mathsf{TrivFib}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-4.72229pt\hskip-18.33684pt\pitchfork\hskip 18.33684pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-18.33684pt\hskip 18.33684pt}}}}
and
\mathsf{TrivCof}={\mathchoice{{\hskip 5.3889pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\smash{\mathsf{Fib}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip-5.3889pt\hskip-18.43057pt\pitchfork\hskip 18.43057pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-18.43057pt\hskip 18.43057pt}}}{{\hskip 5.3889pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\smash{\mathsf{Fib}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip-5.3889pt\hskip-18.43057pt\pitchfork\hskip 18.43057pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-18.43057pt\hskip 18.43057pt}}}{{\hskip 4.72229pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\smash{\mathsf{Fib}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-4.72229pt\hskip-13.36324pt\pitchfork\hskip 13.36324pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-13.36324pt\hskip 13.36324pt}}}{{\hskip 4.72229pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\smash{\mathsf{Fib}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-4.72229pt\hskip-10.14238pt\pitchfork\hskip 10.14238pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-10.14238pt\hskip 10.14238pt}}}}
.
In the following diagram, the first lift is given by the map
\mathsf{Fib}\to\cal{D}_{k}
of
proposition

6.4
and the second lift is given by the unit of the adjunction (
4.1
):

\textstyle{\mathsf{Fib}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathsf{TrivFib}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\smash{\mathsf{Cof}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-19.62503pt\hskip 19.62503pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-19.62503pt\hskip 19.62503pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\smash{\mathsf{Cof}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-19.62503pt\hskip 19.62503pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-19.62503pt\hskip 19.62503pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\smash{\mathsf{Cof}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-14.19934pt\hskip 14.19934pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-14.19934pt\hskip 14.19934pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\smash{\mathsf{Cof}}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-10.73961pt\hskip 10.73961pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-10.73961pt\hskip 10.73961pt}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\cal{E}^{\to}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{\widehat{exp}}(\bar{\delta}^{k},-)}
\textstyle{\cal{E}^{\to}\hbox to0.0pt{.\hss}}

The goal now follows from the outer square by applying
proposition

4.11
as in
remark

4.13
.
∎

7.
The functorial Frobenius property for uniform fibrations

We now continue to consider the fixed suitable awfs
(\mathsf{C},\mathsf{F_{t}})
with generating small category
\cal{I}
and the induced awfs
(\mathsf{C_{t}},\mathsf{F})
, in which the monad algebras for
\mathsf{F}
are exactly the uniform fibrations.
Our aim in this section is to show that
(\mathsf{C_{t}},\mathsf{F})
satisfies the functorial Frobenius property.

For this, we follow a strategy analogous to the one we used in
section

3
.
We begin by organizing strong
k
-oriented homotopy equivalences into a category
\cal{S}_{k}
.
Its objects are 4-tuples
(f,g,\phi,\psi)
consisting of an arrow
f\colon A\to B
together with data
g\colon B\to A
,
\phi\colon I\otimes A\to A
,
\psi\colon I\otimes B\to B
making
f
into a strong
k
-oriented homotopy equivalence in the sense of
definition

3.1
.
A morphism
m\colon(f,g,\phi,\psi)\to(f^{\prime},g^{\prime},\phi^{\prime},\psi^{\prime})
consists of maps
s\colon A\to A^{\prime},t\colon B\to B^{\prime}
such that the following diagrams commute:

\textstyle{A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{s}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{A^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f^{\prime}}
\textstyle{B\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{t}
\textstyle{B^{\prime}\hbox to0.0pt{,\hss}}

\textstyle{B\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{t}
\scriptstyle{g}
\textstyle{B^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g^{\prime}}
\textstyle{A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{s}
\textstyle{A^{\prime}\hbox to0.0pt{,\hss}}

\textstyle{I\otimes A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\phi}
\scriptstyle{I\otimes s}
\textstyle{I\otimes A^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\phi^{\prime}}
\textstyle{A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{s}
\textstyle{A^{\prime}\hbox to0.0pt{,\hss}}

\textstyle{I\otimes B\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\psi}
\scriptstyle{I\otimes t}
\textstyle{I\otimes B^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\psi^{\prime}}
\textstyle{B\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{t}
\textstyle{B^{\prime}\hbox to0.0pt{.\hss}}

There is an obvious first projection functor
p_{k}\colon\cal{S}_{k}\to\cal{E}^{\to}
.

lemma

7.1
below extends the logical equivalence of
lemma

3.3
to an isomorphism of categories.
In its statement, we refer to the maps
\theta^{k}\mathbin{\hat{\otimes}}f\colon f\to\delta^{k}\mathbin{\hat{\otimes}}f
in (
3.3
).

Lemma 7.1
.

For
k\in\{0\,,1\}
, the category
\cal{S}_{k}
of strong
k
-oriented homotopy equivalences can be described isomorphically as the category of arrows
f\in\cal{E}^{\to}
with a retraction
\rho
of
\theta^{k}\mathbin{\hat{\otimes}}f
.
In detail,

(i)

objects are pairs
(f,\rho)
consisting of
f\in\cal{E}^{\to}
and a retraction
\rho
of
\theta^{k}\mathbin{\hat{\otimes}}f
, as below:

\textstyle{f\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\theta^{k}\mathbin{\hat{\otimes}}f}
\textstyle{\delta^{k}\mathbin{\hat{\otimes}}f\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rho}
\textstyle{f\hbox to0.0pt{,\hss}}

(ii)

morphisms
\tau\colon(f,\rho)\to(f^{\prime},\rho^{\prime})
are maps
\tau\colon f\to f^{\prime}
such that the below diagram commutes:

\textstyle{\delta^{k}\mathbin{\hat{\otimes}}f\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rho}
\scriptstyle{\delta^{k}\mathbin{\hat{\otimes}}\tau}
\textstyle{\delta^{k}\mathbin{\hat{\otimes}}f^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rho^{\prime}}
\textstyle{f\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\tau}
\textstyle{f^{\prime}\hbox to0.0pt{.\hss}}

The object part of the correspondence is essentially
lemma

3.3
.
The morphism part is straightforward.
∎

Remark 7.2
.

As suggested by our notation, the maps
\theta^{k}\mathbin{\hat{\otimes}}f\colon f\to\delta^{k}\mathbin{\hat{\otimes}}f
are really given as the action on objects of a natural transformation
\theta^{k}\mathbin{\hat{\otimes}}(-)\colon\operatorname{Id}\to\delta^{k}\mathbin{\hat{\otimes}}(-)
as follows.
Let
\theta^{k}\colon\bot_{\operatorname{Id}}\to\delta^{k}\otimes(-)
denote the arrow in
[\cal{E},\cal{E}^{\to}]\cong[\cal{E},\cal{E}]^{\to}
given by the following square:

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\bot_{\operatorname{Id}}}
\scriptstyle{\bot_{\operatorname{Id}}}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Id}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\delta^{k}\otimes(-)}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Id}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\delta^{1-k}\otimes(-)}
\textstyle{I\otimes(-)\hbox to0.0pt{.\hss}}

Writing
(-)\mathbin{\hat{\otimes}}(-)\colon[\cal{E},\cal{E}]^{\to}\times\cal{E}^{\to}\to\cal{E}^{\to}
for the functor obtained by applying the Leibniz construction to the evaluation functor (
cf.
remark

4.13
), the desired natural transformation is precisely
\theta^{k}\mathbin{\hat{\otimes}}(-)
.

Remark 7.3
.

Recall from
remark

3.2
that the components of the endpoint inclusion
\delta^{k}\otimes(-)
are strong
k
-oriented homotopy equivalences.
In fact, this is the action on objects of a factorization of
\delta^{k}\otimes(-)
through
\cal{S}_{k}
:

\textstyle{\cal{S}_{k}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\cal{E}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\delta^{k}\otimes(-)}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\delta^{k}\otimes(-)}}
\textstyle{\cal{E}^{\to}\hbox to0.0pt{.\hss}}

The next lemma is the analogue of
lemma

3.4
.

Lemma 7.4
.

For
k\in\{0\,,1\}
, the functor
\delta^{k}\mathbin{\hat{\otimes}}(-)\colon\cal{E}^{\to}\to\cal{E}^{\to}
factors via
\cal{S}_{k}
as follows:

\textstyle{\cal{S}_{k}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\cal{E}^{\to}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\delta^{k}\mathbin{\hat{\otimes}}(-)}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\delta^{k}\mathbin{\hat{\otimes}}(-)}\,}
\textstyle{\cal{E}^{\to}\hbox to0.0pt{.\hss}}

We give a proof using the formalism of Leibniz constructions, as explained in
[
36
]
.
For this, we use the description of
\cal{S}_{k}
provided by
lemma

7.1
and the notation of
remark

7.2
, thus writing simply
\delta^{k}
for the natural transformation
\delta^{k}\otimes(-)
.
We apply the Leibniz construction to the endofunctor composition functor
(-)\mathbin{\circ}(-)\colon[\cal{E},\cal{E}]\times[\cal{E},\cal{E}]\to[\cal{E},\cal{E}]
, so as to obtain a functor

(-)\mathbin{\hat{\mathbin{\circ}}}(-)\colon[\cal{E},\cal{E}]^{\to}\times[\cal{E},\cal{E}]^{\to}\to[\cal{E},\cal{E}]^{\to}.

Recall from
remark

7.3
that
\delta^{k}\colon\cal{E}\to\cal{E}^{\to}
factors via
\cal{S}_{k}\to\cal{E}^{\to}
,
i.e.
that the arrow

\theta^{k}\mathbin{\hat{\otimes}}(\delta^{k}\otimes X)\colon\delta^{k}\otimes X\to\delta^{k}\mathbin{\hat{\otimes}}(\delta^{k}\otimes X)

exhibits
\delta^{k}\otimes X
as a retract of
\delta^{k}\mathbin{\hat{\otimes}}(\delta^{k}\otimes X)
, functorially in
X\in\cal{E}
.
This amounts to saying that the map of natural transformations

\theta^{k}\mathbin{\hat{\mathbin{\circ}}}\delta^{k}\colon\delta^{k}\to\delta^{k}\mathbin{\hat{\mathbin{\circ}}}\delta^{k}

exhibits
\delta^{k}
as a retract of
\delta^{k}\mathbin{\hat{\mathbin{\circ}}}\delta^{k}
in
[\cal{E},\cal{E}]^{\to}
.

Our goal is to show that
\delta^{k}\mathbin{\hat{\otimes}}(-)\colon\cal{E}^{\to}\to\cal{E}^{\to}
can be factored via
\cal{S}_{k}\to\cal{E}^{\to}
.
For this, we need to show that the arrow

\theta^{k}\mathbin{\hat{\otimes}}(\delta^{k}\mathbin{\hat{\otimes}}f)\colon\delta^{k}\mathbin{\hat{\otimes}}f\to\delta^{k}\mathbin{\hat{\otimes}}(\delta^{k}\mathbin{\hat{\otimes}}f)

exhibits
\delta^{k}\mathbin{\hat{\otimes}}f
as a retract of
\delta^{k}\mathbin{\hat{\otimes}}(\delta^{k}\mathbin{\hat{\otimes}}f)
, functorially in
f\in\cal{E}^{\to}
.
But associativity of functor composition implies associativity of the corresponding Leibniz construction (since the involved functors preserve pushouts), and therefore we can restate our goal as asserting that the map

(\theta^{k}\mathbin{\hat{\mathbin{\circ}}}\delta^{k})\mathbin{\hat{\otimes}}f\colon\delta^{k}\mathbin{\hat{\otimes}}f\to(\delta^{k}\mathbin{\hat{\mathbin{\circ}}}\delta^{k})\mathbin{\hat{\otimes}}f

exhibits
\delta^{k}\mathbin{\hat{\otimes}}f
as a retract of
(\delta^{k}\mathbin{\hat{\mathbin{\circ}}}\delta^{k})\mathbin{\hat{\otimes}}f
, functorially in
f\in\cal{E}^{\to}
.
But functors, in this case the functor
(-)\mathbin{\hat{\otimes}}(-)\colon[\cal{E},\cal{E}]^{\to}\times\cal{E}^{\to}\to\cal{E}^{\to}
mapping
(\alpha,f)
to
\alpha\mathbin{\hat{\otimes}}f
, preserve section-retraction pairs.
∎

The next lemma is the analogue of
lemma

3.5
.
Define
\cal{S}=_{\operatorname{def}}\cal{S}_{0}+\cal{S}_{1}
.

Proposition 7.5
.

(i)

There is a functor

\textstyle{\cal{I}_{\otimes}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{u_{\otimes}}
\scriptstyle{F}
\textstyle{\mathsf{Cof}\times_{\cal{E}^{\to}}\cal{S}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\cal{E}^{\to}\hbox to0.0pt{.\hss}}

(ii)

There is a functor

\textstyle{\mathsf{Cof}\times_{\cal{E}^{\to}}\cal{S}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{H}
\textstyle{\mathsf{TrivCof}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\cal{E}^{\to}\hbox to0.0pt{.\hss}}

We begin by considering (i).
For
k\in\{0\,,1\}
, we show that there is a functor

\textstyle{\cal{I}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\delta^{k}\mathbin{\hat{\otimes}}u}
\scriptstyle{M_{k}}
\textstyle{\mathsf{Cof}\times_{\cal{E}^{\to}}\cal{S}_{k}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\cal{E}^{\to}\hbox to0.0pt{.\hss}}

The map to the first factor is given by the embedding
\cal{I}\to\mathsf{Cof}
and the assumption that
\mathsf{Cof}
is closed under Leibniz product with the endpoint inclusions, which is condition (S4) of
definition

6.1
.
The map to the second factor is given by
lemma

7.4
.
The claim in (i) then follows by combining the cases
k=0
and
k=1
.

For (ii), let us write
c\colon\mathsf{Cof}\to\cal{E}^{\to}
for the forgetful functor.
We fix again
k\in\{0\,,1\}
and show that there is a functor

(7.1)

\begin{gathered}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 23.93411pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-23.93411pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathsf{Cof}\times_{\cal{E}^{\to}}\cal{S}_{k}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 27.93408pt\raise-25.16312pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 17.92639pt\raise 5.92223pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.86113pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{N_{k}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 47.80908pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 32.8716pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 47.80908pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\overline{\mathsf{Cof}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise-32.30222pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 27.93408pt\raise-32.30222pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\cal{E}^{\to}\hbox to0.0pt{.\hss}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{gathered}

We only describe the action of the functor
N_{k}
on an object
(i,\rho)
with
i\in\mathsf{Cof}
and
\rho
a retraction of
\theta^{k}\mathbin{\hat{\otimes}}c_{i}\colon c_{i}\to\delta^{k}\mathbin{\hat{\otimes}}c_{i}
, leaving the evident definition of the action on arrows to the reader.
Since
\rho
exhibits
c_{i}
as a retract of
\delta^{k}\mathbin{\hat{\otimes}}c_{i}
, we may define
N_{k}(i,\rho)=_{\operatorname{def}}(i,c_{i},\theta^{k}\mathbin{\hat{\otimes}}c_{i},\rho)
.
Observe that this definition makes the diagram for
N_{k}
commute.
We then pass from
\overline{\delta^{k}\mathbin{\hat{\otimes}}c}\colon\overline{\mathsf{Cof}}\to\cal{E}^{\to}
to
\overline{\mathsf{TrivCof}}
using
corollary

6.7
.
Upon combining the cases
k=0
and
k=1
, this gives a functor
\mathsf{Cof}\times_{\cal{E}^{\to}}\cal{S}\to\overline{\mathsf{TrivCof}}
over
\cal{E}^{\to}
.
The claim in (ii) then follows by
proposition

4.2
and the adjunction (
4.1
), noting that
\mathsf{TrivCof}
is a category of left maps.
∎

Remark 7.6
.

We also have a pair of functors
{\mathchoice{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\smash{(\mathsf{Cof}\times_{\cal{E}^{\to}}\cal{S})}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-50.49394pt\hskip 50.49394pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-50.49394pt\hskip 50.49394pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\smash{(\mathsf{Cof}\times_{\cal{E}^{\to}}\cal{S})}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-50.49394pt\hskip 50.49394pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-50.49394pt\hskip 50.49394pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\smash{(\mathsf{Cof}\times_{\cal{E}^{\to}}\cal{S})}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-40.58678pt\hskip 40.58678pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-40.58678pt\hskip 40.58678pt}}}{{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\smash{(\mathsf{Cof}\times_{\cal{E}^{\to}}\cal{S})}\vphantom{I}}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-35.02846pt\hskip 35.02846pt\pitchfork}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-35.02846pt\hskip 35.02846pt}}}}\leftrightarrow\mathsf{Fib}
relating uniform fibrations with right
(\mathsf{Cof}\times_{\cal{E}^{\to}}\cal{S})
-maps.
This means that a map can be equipped with the structure of a uniform fibration if and only if it can be equipped with the structure of a right
(\mathsf{Cof}\times_{\cal{E}^{\to}}\cal{S})
-map.
This is the analogue of the equality between sets in
remark

3.6
.
However, these functors do not in general form an equivalence.

We are now ready to show that the awfs in which the right maps are the uniform fibrations (constructed in
theorem

6.5
) satisfies the functorial Frobenius property.
For this, we use our characterisation of the functorial Frobenius property in algebraically-free awfs’s stated in
theorem

5.9
.
As the main step, we show that that strong homotopy equivalences and uniform fibrations satisfy the generalized functorial Frobenius condition,
i.e.
the algebraic analogue of
lemma

3.7
.

Lemma 7.7
.

The triple
(\cal{S},\mathsf{Fib},\cal{S})
satisfies the generalized functorial Frobenius condition.

The core of the argument is to obtain, for
k\in\{0\,,1\}
, a lift

in

) and
